\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\infty}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q(1-q)^{k-1}e_{k,\lambda}(x-u_{1}-\cdots-u_{k})\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}F_{U}(du_{i}).

By (4.14) and (4.15) in
[
41
, p.156]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q(1-q)^{k-1}e_{k,\lambda}(x-u_{1}-\cdots-u_{k})\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q(1-q)^{k-1}e_{k,\lambda}(x)=\frac{q}{\lambda},

and hence the density function of the geometric sum is uniformly bounded by
q/\lambda
.

\Box

Lemma 4.4

The following two upper bounds hold uniformly in
t\geq 0
and
y>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}R_{\varPi_{n}}(t)\leq\frac{1}{c}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sup_{n\geq 1}R_{n}(t,y)\leq 1+\frac{t\wedge y}{c}.

(4.23)

Proof.
Here we just prove the first upper bound.
The second one is a direct consequence of the first one.
By (
3.4
) and the first equality in (
4.14
), the resolvent
R_{\varPi_{n}}
has the following expansion

\displaystyle R_{\varPi_{n}}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}}{1-\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\big(1-\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}\big)\big(\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}\big)^{k-1}\cdot\big(\pi^{*}_{n}\big)^{(*k)}(t).

(4.24)

By (
4.9
) and (
4.14
), we have
\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}<1
and
\pi^{*}_{n}(y)=\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\mu_{n}(y)
with

\mu_{n}(dy):=p_{n}\cdot\delta_{0}(dy)+(1-p_{n})\cdot\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*}_{n}(y)\,dy
for
y\geq 0
.
Hence the sum on the right side of (
4.24
) is the probability density function of the geometric sum in Proposition
4.3
with
q=1-\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}
,
\lambda=c
and
U_{i}
distributed as
\mu_{n}(dy)
.
Hence

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}R_{\varPi_{n}}(t)\leq\frac{\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}}{1-\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}}\cdot\frac{1-\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}}{c}=\frac{\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}}{c}\leq\frac{1}{c},

uniformly in
n\geq 1
.

\Box

In the next lemma, we prove the weak convergence of measures with densities
R_{\varPi_{n}}(n\cdot)
and
R_{n}(n\cdot,y)
by considering the aysmptotics of their Laplace transforms.

Lemma 4.5

For each
\beta\geq 0
and
y\geq 0
, we have as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta s}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\overset{\rm u.c.}{\to}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta s}W^{\prime}(s)\,ds\quad\mbox{and}\quad\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta s}R_{n}(ns,y)\,ds\overset{\rm u.c.}{\to}y\cdot\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta s}W^{\prime}(s)\,ds.

Proof.
Denote by
\mathcal{L}_{R_{\varPi_{n}}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\varPi}_{n}}
the Laplace transforms of
R_{\varPi_{n}}
and
\overline{\varPi}_{n}
respectively.
Taking Laplace transforms on both sides of (
4.21
) shows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{R_{\varPi_{n}}}(\lambda)=\gamma_{n}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\varPi}_{n}}(\lambda)+\gamma_{n}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\varPi}_{n}}(\lambda)\cdot\mathcal{L}_{R_{\varPi_{n}}}(\lambda)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{L}_{R_{\varPi_{n}}}(\lambda)=\frac{\gamma_{n}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\varPi}_{n}}(\lambda)}{1-\gamma_{n}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\varPi}_{n}}(\lambda)},\quad\lambda>0,

which along with the change of variables induces that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)\,dt=\frac{1}{n}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{R_{\varPi_{n}}}(\lambda/n)=\frac{\gamma_{n}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\varPi}_{n}}(\lambda/n)}{n\big(1-\gamma_{n}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\varPi}_{n}}(\lambda/n)\big)}.

(4.25)

By using the dominated convergence theorem and then (
4.9
), we first have as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{n}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\varPi}_{n}}(\lambda/n)\sim\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}\to 1.

Moreover, by using integration by parts to the denominator of the last fraction in (
4.25
),

\displaystyle n\big(1-\gamma_{n}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\varPi}_{n}}(\lambda/n)\big)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{n^{2}}{\lambda}\Big(\frac{\lambda}{n}-\gamma_{n}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda}{n}e^{-\frac{\lambda}{n}t}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\Big)

(4.26)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{n^{2}}{\lambda}\Big(\frac{\lambda}{n}+\gamma_{n}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(1-e^{-\frac{\lambda}{n}t}\big)\,\overline{\varPi}_{n}(dt)\Big)=\frac{\varPhi^{(n)}(\lambda)}{\lambda},

(4.27)

which goes to
\varPhi(\lambda)/\lambda
as
n\to\infty
by Proposition
4.2
.
Plugging these back into (
4.25
) gives that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)\,dt=\frac{\lambda}{\varPhi(\lambda)}=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}W^{\prime}(t)\,dt,

(4.28)

which induces the weak convergence of measures with density functions
\{R_{\varPi_{n}}(n\cdot)\}_{n\geq 1}
to the measure with density
W^{\prime}
and hence the first desired locally uniform convergence holds.
Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}R_{n}(nt,y)\,dt=\frac{\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\lambda}{n}t}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{y>t\}}\,dt}{n\big(1-\gamma_{n}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\varPi}_{n}}(\lambda/n)\big)}\to y\cdot\frac{\lambda}{\varPhi(\lambda)}=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\lambda t}\cdot y\cdot W^{\prime}(t)\,dt,

as
n\to\infty
and the second desired locally uniform convergence also holds.

\Box

Corollary 4.6

We have
W^{\prime}(t)\leq 1/c
for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.
The definition of
W^{\prime}
first tells that
W^{\prime}(t)=0
for any
t<0
.
By Lemma
4.5
, the
\sigma
-finite measure on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
with density
R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)
converges vaguely as
n\to\infty
to the
\sigma
-finite measure with density
W^{\prime}(t)
, which along with Lemma
4.4
induces that the
\sigma
-finite measure on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
with density
1/c-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)
converges vaguely as
n\to\infty
to the
\sigma
-finite measure with density
1/c-W^{\prime}(t)
that is non-negative almost everywhere.
Finally, the continuity of
W^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
yields that
W^{\prime}(t)\leq 1/c
for all
t\geq 0
.

\Box

The lack of monotonicity makes the proof of point-wise convergence of
R_{\varPi_{n}}
and
R_{n}
to the corresponding limits quite challenging or even impossible.
The next best thing we can do is to establish their
L^{p}
-convergence with the help of the following two uniform bound estimates for the Fourier transforms of
\{\overline{\varPi}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
.

Proposition 4.7

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
\beta,y\geq 0
and
z\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\bigg|\leq\frac{C}{|z|}\wedge 1\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sup_{n\geq 1}\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{y>t\}}\,dt\bigg|\leq\frac{C}{|z|}\wedge y.

(4.29)

Proof.
By (
4.9
), we first have uniformly in
n\geq 1
,
z\in\mathbb{R}
and
\beta\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\bigg|\leq\gamma_{n}\cdot\big\|\overline{\varPi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}\leq 1.

(4.30)

Moreover, the differentiation property of Fourier transform induces that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{n}}{z}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\big(e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\big)\,dt

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma_{n}}{z}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\Big(\frac{\beta}{n}+\pi_{n}(t)\Big)\,dt,

which along with
\gamma_{n}\leq 1/c
induces that

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\bigg|\leq\frac{\gamma_{n}}{|z|}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\Big(\frac{\beta}{n}+\pi_{n}(t)\Big)\,dt\leq\frac{2}{c\cdot|z|}.

This together with (
4.30
) yields the first upper bound in (
4.29
).
For the second one, we also have

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{y>t\}}\,dt\bigg|\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{y>t\}}\,dt=y.

Additionally, integration by parts gives that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{y>t\}}\,dt=\frac{e^{(\mathtt{i}z-\frac{\beta}{n})y}-1}{\mathtt{i}z-\beta/n},

and then

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{y>t\}}\,dt\bigg|\leq\sup_{n\geq 1}\frac{2}{|\mathtt{i}z-\beta/n|}\leq\frac{2}{|z|}.

Consequently, the second upper bound in (
4.29
) holds.

\Box

Proposition 4.8

There exists a constant
\beta_{0}\geq 0
such that for each
\beta\geq\beta_{0}
, we can always find two constants
n_{0}\geq 1
and
T_{0}>0
satisfying that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq n_{0}}\int_{T_{0}}^{\infty}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\leq\frac{c}{8}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\inf_{n\geq n_{0}}\int_{0}^{T_{0}}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\geq\frac{3\cdot c}{4}.

(4.31)

Proof.
Firstly, a simple calculation along with (
4.6
) induces that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\geq\gamma_{n}c\int_{0}^{T}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(t)\,dt\geq\frac{\gamma_{n}}{(1/c+\beta/n)^{2}}\Big(1-e^{-\frac{T}{c}}-\frac{T}{c}e^{-\frac{T}{c}}\Big)\to c,

as
n,T\to\infty
, and also by the fact that
\gamma_{n}\leq 1/c
,

\displaystyle\int_{T}^{\infty}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{T}^{\infty}t\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(t)\,dt+\frac{\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{0}^{\infty}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(t)\,dt

(4.32)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle(c+T)e^{-T/c}+\frac{\theta_{n}\big\|\overline{\Lambda}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}}{c}\int_{0}^{\infty}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*\frac{\overline{\Lambda}_{n}}{\big\|\overline{\Lambda}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}}(t)\,dt.

(4.33)

Let
X
and
Y
be two independent random variables with density
\boldsymbol{e}_{c}
and
\big\|\overline{\Lambda}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}^{-1}\cdot\overline{\Lambda}_{n}
respectively. The second term on the right side of equality in (
4.32
) equals to

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{n}\big\|\overline{\Lambda}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}}{c}\mathbf{E}\Big[(X+Y)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(X+Y)}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{n}\big\|\overline{\Lambda}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}}{c}\mathbf{E}\big[X\big]+\frac{\theta_{n}\big\|\overline{\Lambda}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}}{c}\mathbf{E}\big[Ye^{-\frac{\beta}{n}Y}\big]

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\theta_{n}\big\|\overline{\Lambda}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}+\frac{\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{0}^{\infty}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(t)\,dt.

By (
4.1
), the change of variables, the identity
\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n})=c^{-1}\cdot n^{2}\theta_{n}
and then integration by parts,

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{0}^{\infty}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(t)\,dt\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}te^{-\beta t}\overline{\nu}(t)\,dt=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}se^{-\beta s}\,ds\,\nu(dt)\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{t^{2}}{2}\wedge\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\Big)\,\nu(dt).

Taking these estimates back into the right side of the equality in (
4.32
) yields that

\displaystyle\int_{T}^{\infty}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\gamma_{n}\cdot\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle(c+T)e^{-T/c}+\theta_{n}\big\|\overline{\Lambda}_{n}\big\|_{L^{1}}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{t^{2}}{2}\wedge\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}\Big)\,\nu(dt)\to 0,

(4.34)

as
n\to\infty
and then
\beta,T\to\infty
by using the dominated convergence theorem along with (
1.2
), (
4.2
) and Proposition
4.1
.
Hence the desired uniform upper and lower bounds hold.

\Box

Proposition 4.9

For each
\beta>\beta_{0}
, there exist constants
n_{0}\geq 1
and
C\geq 0
such that for any
z\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\inf_{n\geq n_{0}}\bigg|1-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\bigg|\geq C\cdot\big(|z|\wedge 1\big).

(4.35)

Proof.
Note that
\cos(x)\geq 1/2
for any
|x|\leq 1
.
The two bound estimates in (
4.31
) yield that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\int_{0}^{\infty}\sin(zt)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\cos(zt)\cdot te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt

\displaystyle\geq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T_{0}}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt-\int_{T_{0}}^{\infty}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt,

which is larger or equal to
c/4
for
|z|\leq 1/T_{0}
.
This along with the mean value theorem induces that

\displaystyle\bigg|1-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\bigg|\geq\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}\sin(zt)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\bigg|\geq\frac{c}{4}\cdot|z|.

Hence the desired inequality (
4.35
) holds for any
|z|\leq 1/T_{0}
.

For
|z|>1/T_{0}
, from Proposition
4.7
, there exists a constant
z_{0}>0
such that for any
|z|\geq z_{0}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\bigg|\leq\frac{1}{2}\quad\mbox{and hence}\quad\inf_{n\geq 1}\bigg|1-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\bigg|\geq\frac{1}{2}.

Obviously, the desired inequality (
4.35
) holds for any
z\in\mathbb{R}
if
1/T_{0}\geq z_{0}
and then the proof ends.
It remains to consider the case
1/T_{0}<z_{0}
.
Actually, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\inf_{|z|>1/T_{0}}\inf_{n\geq n_{0}}\Big|1-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\Big|\geq C_{0},

(4.36)

for some constant
C_{0}>0
.
Notice that

\displaystyle\bigg|1-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\bigg|\geq 1-F_{n}(z)\quad\mbox{with}\quad F_{n}(z)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\cos(zt)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt.

The continuity of
F_{n}
guarantees that its local maximum on the interval
[1/T_{0},z_{0}]
can be attained at some point
z_{n}\in[1/T_{0},z_{0}]
.
For any
T>0
, the fact that
\cos(x)\leq 1
induces that

\displaystyle F_{n}(z_{n})\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\cos(z_{n}t)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt+\int_{T}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt.

(4.37)

Using (
4.34
) again, we can choose
T>0
large enough such that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq n_{0}}\int_{T}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\leq\frac{1}{T}\cdot\sup_{n\geq n_{0}}\int_{T}^{\infty}te^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\leq\frac{1}{2}

and hence

\displaystyle\inf_{n\geq n_{0}}\int_{0}^{T}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\geq\frac{1}{2}.

By the periodicity of
\cos(z_{n}t)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\cos(z_{n}t)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{[Tz_{n}/(2\pi)]}\int_{(2k\pi-\pi/2)/z_{n}}^{{(2k\pi+\pi/2)/z_{n}}}\cos(z_{n}t)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt.

(4.38)

We now start to analyze the maximum of the sum above.
Notice that
\cos(z_{n}t)
is unimodal on each interval
[(2k\pi-\pi/2)/z_{n},(2k\pi+\pi/2)/z_{n}]
for any
k\geq 0
with the maximum arrived at the point
2k\pi/z_{n}
.
Thus the more weight of
e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\gamma_{n}\cdot\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)
is distributed around the local maximum points, the larger the sum above will be.
To obtain the maximum of the summation in (
4.38
) we should split the weight of
\int_{0}^{T}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\gamma_{n}\cdot\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)dt
uniformly around these maximum points.
More precisely, we can choose
T>0
large enough such that

\displaystyle R_{z_{n}}:=\frac{z_{n}\int_{0}^{T}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt}{2\gamma_{n}\cdot([Tz_{n}/(2\pi)]+1)}<1.

From the previous observation and the fact that
\cos(z_{n}t)\leq 1
and
\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\leq 1
, we have for any
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{(2k\pi-\pi/2)/z_{n}}^{{(2k\pi+\pi/2)/z_{n}}}\cos(z_{n}t)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\gamma_{n}\int_{(2k\pi-R_{z_{n}})/z_{n}}^{{(2k\pi+R_{z_{n}})/z_{n}}}\cos(z_{n}t)\,dt

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{0}^{T}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)dt}{[Tz_{n}/(2\pi)]+1}\cdot\frac{\sin(R_{z_{n}})}{R_{z_{n}}}.

Taking this back into (
4.38
) and then (
4.37
), we have

\displaystyle F_{n}(z_{n})\leq\frac{\sin(R_{z_{n}})}{R_{z_{n}}}\int_{0}^{T}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt+\int_{T}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt

and hence

\displaystyle\inf_{|z|>1/T_{0}}\Big|1-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{\mathtt{i}zt}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\gamma_{n}\cdot\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\Big|\!\!\!

\displaystyle\geq\!\!\!

\displaystyle 1-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt

\displaystyle+\Big(1-\frac{\sin(R_{z_{n}})}{R_{z_{n}}}\Big)\cdot\int_{0}^{T}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt

\displaystyle\geq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big(1-\frac{\sin(R_{z_{n}})}{R_{z_{n}}}\Big).

By the fact that
z_{n}\in[1/T_{0},z_{0}]
and
\gamma_{n}<1
for any
n\geq n_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\geq n_{0}}R_{z_{n}}>0\quad\mbox{and hence}\quad\sup_{n\geq n_{0}}\frac{\sin(R_{z_{n}})}{R_{z_{n}}}<1.

Consequently, the uniform lower bound (
4.36
) holds.

\Box

Lemma 4.10

For any
\beta\geq\beta_{0}
and
y>0
, we have as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-2\beta t}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)-W^{\prime}(t)\big|^{2}dt+\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-2\beta t}\big|R_{n}(nt,y)-y\cdot W^{\prime}(t)\big|^{2}dt\to 0

Proof.
Here we just prove the convergence to
0
of the first integral.
The second one can be proved in the same way.
We first give some upper bounds for the Fourier transform of
R^{(n)}_{\varPi}(n\cdot)
.
By (
4.28
),

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)\,dt\bigg|\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)\,dt\to\frac{\beta}{\varPhi(\beta)}>0,

as
n\to\infty
, which immediately yields that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)\,dt\Big|<\infty.

Similarly as in the proof of Lemma
4.5
, we also have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(t)\,dt=\frac{\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)t}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt}{1-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)t}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt},\quad z\in\mathbb{R}.

By the change of variables and then using Proposition
4.7
and
4.9
,
there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
z\in\mathbb{R}
and large
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)\,dt\bigg|=\frac{\big|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)\cdot\frac{t}{n}}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\big|}{n\cdot\big|1-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)\cdot\frac{t}{n}}\cdot\gamma_{n}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t)\,dt\big|}\leq C\cdot\frac{\frac{n}{|z|}\wedge 1}{|z|\wedge n}=\frac{C}{|z|}.

Putting these estimates together, there exist two constants constants
C>0
and
n_{0}\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq n_{0}}\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)dt\Big|\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\Big(\frac{1}{|z|}\wedge 1\Big),

(4.39)

uniformly in
z\in\mathbb{R}
.
By using the Fourier isometry along with the square integrability of
e^{-\beta t}\cdot R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)
and
e^{-\beta t}\cdot W^{\prime}(t)
as well as (
2.4
),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-2\beta t}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)-W^{\prime}(t)\big|^{2}dt=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)dt-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)t}W^{\prime}(t)dt\bigg|^{2}dz,

which goes to
0
as
n\to\infty
by the dominated convergence theorem and (
4.28
) with
\lambda=\beta-\mathrm{i}z
.

\Box

Corollary 4.11

For any
T\geq 0
and
y>0
, we have as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)-W^{\prime}(t)\big|^{2}\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\big|R_{n}(nt,y)-y\cdot W^{\prime}(t)\big|^{2}\,dt\to 0.

(4.40)

Lemma 4.12

For any
\beta\geq\beta_{0}
, there exist two constants
C>0
and
n_{0}\geq 1
such that for any
\delta\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq n_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|e^{-\beta(t+\delta)}R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t+\delta)\big)-e^{-\beta t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)\big|^{2}\,dt\leq C\cdot\delta.

Proof.
By the Fourier isometry, the square integrability of
e^{-\beta t}\cdot R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)
and then the change of variables,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|e^{-\beta(t+\delta)}R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t+\delta)\big)-e^{-\beta t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)\big|^{2}\,dt=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big|\big(e^{\mathrm{i}z\delta}-1\big)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{(\mathrm{i}z-\beta)t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)\,dt\Big|^{2}dz.

An application of (
4.39
) and the inequality
\big|e^{\mathrm{i}z}-1\big|\leq|z|\wedge 2
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
to the right-hand side yields that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq n_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|e^{-\beta(t+\delta)}R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t+\delta)\big)-e^{-\beta t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)\big|^{2}\,dt\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big((z\delta)^{2}\wedge 1\big)\Big(\frac{1}{z^{2}}\wedge 1\Big)dz\leq C\cdot\delta,

uniformly in
\delta>0
.

\Box

The next corollary can be proved similarly as Corollary
2.3
by using the preceding lemma.

Corollary 4.13

For each
T\geq 0
, there exist two constants
C>0
and
n_{0}\geq 1
such that for any
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq n_{0}}\int_{-\infty}^{T}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt)\big|^{2}\,dt\leq C\cdot\delta.

4.2.2
Uniform moment estimates

In this section, we mainly establish a uniform upper bound for all moments of the sequence
\{X^{(n)}_{\zeta}\}_{n\geq 1}
with the help of the following moment results for the measures
\Lambda_{n}(dy)
and
\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(dy)
as well as the random variable
\ell^{\Lambda}_{n,1}
.

Proposition 4.14

For each
p\geq 2
, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
n
and
t
such that

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(t\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{p}\Lambda_{n}(dy)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot(1+t)^{p-1},

(4.41)

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(t\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{p}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\Big(\frac{\theta_{n}}{n^{p-2}}+(1+t)^{p-1}\Big).

(4.42)

Proof.
By (
4.1
) and then the change of variables and Fubini’s theorem, we first have

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(t\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{p}\,\Lambda_{n}(dy)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle c\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(t\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{p}\,\nu\Big(\eta_{n}+\frac{dy}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle c\int_{0}^{\infty}(t\wedge y)^{p}\,\nu(\eta_{n}+dy)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle cp\int_{0}^{t}y^{p-1}\cdot\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle cp\int_{0}^{t}y^{p-1}\cdot\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy,

uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
t\geq 0
, which, by (
1.2
), can be uniformly bounded by

\displaystyle C\Big(\int_{0}^{1}y^{p-1}\cdot\bar{\nu}(y)dy+\int_{1}^{1\vee t}y^{p-1}\cdot\bar{\nu}(y)dy\Big)\leq C\Big(1+t^{p-1}\cdot\int_{1}^{\infty}\bar{\nu}(y)dy\Big)\leq C\cdot(1+t)^{p-1}

and then (
4.41
) holds.
By the inequality
(x+y)\wedge t\leq x+y\wedge t
for
x,y\geq 0
and the power inequality,

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(t\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{p}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(t\wedge\frac{x+y}{n}\Big)^{p}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(x)\,dx\Lambda_{n}(dy)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{x}{n}\Big)^{p}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(x)\,dx+C\cdot n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(t\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{p}\,\Lambda_{n}(dy),

which along with the first result induces (
4.42
) immediately.

\Box

Corollary 4.15

For any
p\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
n
and
t
such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|R_{n}(nt,y)\big|^{p}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\leq C\quad\mbox{and}\quad\frac{\theta_{n}}{n^{p-2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|R_{n}(nt,y)\big|^{p}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\leq C\cdot(1+t)^{p}.

Proof.
By using the second inequality in (
4.23
) and then the power inequality,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|R_{n}(nt,y)\big|^{p}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy+C\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}y^{p}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy<\infty

and the first desired inequality holds.
Similarly, the second one can be proved by using (
4.42
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{n}}{n^{p-2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|R_{n}(nt,y)\big|^{p}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{n}}{n^{p-2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(1+\frac{(nt)\wedge y}{c}\Big)^{p}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy

(4.43)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\frac{\theta_{n}}{n^{p-2}}+C\cdot n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(t\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{p}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy,

(4.44)

which can be bounded by
C\cdot(1+t)^{p}
uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
t\geq 0
.

\Box

Proposition 4.16

For each
p\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
n
and
t
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\bigg[\Big|\frac{\ell^{\Lambda}_{n,1}}{n}\wedge t\Big|^{p}\bigg]\leq C\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{n^{p}}+\frac{t^{p-1}}{n(1-p_{n})}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}(y\wedge t)\cdot\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy\bigg).

Proof.
Similarly as in (
4.43
), we have uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\bigg[\Big|\frac{\ell^{\Lambda}_{n,1}}{n}\wedge t\Big|^{p}\bigg]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{p}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy+C\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{y}{n}\wedge t\Big)^{p}\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*}_{n}(y)\,dy.

The first term on right side of this equality can be uniformly bounded by
C\cdot n^{-p}
.
For the second term, by using (
4.3
) and then the change of variables and (
4.15
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{y}{n}\wedge t\Big)^{p}\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*}_{n}(y)\,dy\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\int_{0}^{\infty}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+z)\,dz}\int_{0}^{\infty}(y\wedge t)^{p}\cdot\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)dy

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{p_{n}}{n(1-p_{n})}\int_{0}^{\infty}(y\wedge t)^{p}\cdot\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n(1-p_{n})}\int_{0}^{\infty}(y\wedge t)^{p}\cdot\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{t^{p-1}}{n(1-p_{n})}\int_{0}^{\infty}(y\wedge t)\cdot\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy.

Here the last integral is finite because of (
1.2
).
The desired inequality holds.

\Box

Corollary 4.17

For each
p\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
n
and
t
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|R_{n}(nt,\ell^{c}_{n,1})\big|^{p}\Big]\leq C\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|R_{n}(nt,\ell^{\Lambda}_{n,1})\big|^{p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\frac{n^{p-1}}{1-p_{n}}\cdot(1+t)^{p}.

Proof.
By (
4.23
) and the power inequality, we have uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|R_{n}(nt,\ell^{c}_{n,1})\big|^{p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\Big(1+\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\ell^{c}_{n,1}\big|^{p}\Big]\Big)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|R_{n}(nt,\ell^{\Lambda}_{n,1})\big|^{p}\Big]\leq C\Big(1+\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\ell^{\Lambda}_{n,1}\wedge(nt)\big|^{p}\Big]\Big).

The two desired inequalities follow respectively from

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\ell^{c}_{n,1}\big|^{p}\Big]<\infty\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\ell^{\Lambda}_{n,1}\wedge(nt)\big|^{p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\frac{n^{p-1}}{1-p_{n}}\cdot(1+t)^{p},

uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
t\geq 0
;
see Proposition
4.16
.

\Box

Proposition 4.18

For each
p\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
\zeta,t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1}(t)\big|^{p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\zeta^{p}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2}(t)\big|^{p}\Big]\leq C\cdot(\zeta\vee\zeta^{p})\cdot\big(1+t\big)^{p}.

(4.45)

Proof.
By Hölder’s inequality, it suffices to prove this proposition for
p=k\in\mathbb{N}
. By using the fact
B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)\leq[n\zeta]
a.s., Jensen’s inequality and then the mutual-independence among
\{\ell_{n,j}^{c}\}_{j\geq 1}

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1}(t)\big|^{k}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\zeta^{k}\cdot\sup_{n\geq 1}\cdot\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{[n\zeta]}\sum_{j=1}^{[n\zeta]}R_{n}(nt,\ell_{n,j}^{c})\bigg|^{k}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\zeta^{k}\cdot\sup_{n\geq 1}\cdot\frac{1}{[n\zeta]}\sum_{j=1}^{[n\zeta]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|R_{n}(nt,\ell_{n,j}^{c})\big|^{k}\Big]

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\zeta^{k}\cdot\sup_{n\geq 1}\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|R_{n}(nt,\ell_{n,1}^{c})\big|^{k}\Big],

which is bounded by
C\cdot\zeta^{k}
uniformly in
t\geq 0
; see Corollary
4.17
, and hence the first desired uniform upper bound holds.
For the second one, without loss of generality we assume that
[n\zeta]>k
.
By repeating the binomial expansion,

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}R_{n}(nt,\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda})\bigg|^{k}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{k}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k-k_{1}}\cdots\sum_{k_{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}=0}^{k-\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)-1}k_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}{{k-\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}k_{i}}\choose{k_{j}}}\Big|R_{n}(nt,\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda})\Big|^{k_{j}}.

Taking expectations on both sides of this equality conditionally on
B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)
and then using Corollary
4.17
, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
n
and
t
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}R_{n}(nt,\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda})\bigg|^{k}\,\Bigg|\,B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{k}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k-k_{1}}\cdots\sum_{k_{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}=0}^{k-\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)-1}k_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}{{k-\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}k_{i}}\choose{k_{j}}}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|R_{n}(nt,\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda})\big|^{k_{j}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{k}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k-k_{1}}\cdots\sum_{k_{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}=0}^{k-\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)-1}k_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}{{k-\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}k_{i}}\choose{k_{j}}}\frac{\big(n(1-p_{n})\big)^{(k_{j}-1)^{+}}}{|1-p_{n}|^{k_{j}}}(1+t)^{k_{j}}

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{k}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k-k_{1}}\cdots\sum_{k_{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}=0}^{k-\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)-1}k_{i}}\cdot\frac{\big(n(1-p_{n})\big)^{k-J}}{|1-p_{n}|^{k}}\cdot(1+t)^{k}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}{{k-\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}k_{i}}\choose{k_{j}}}

with
J:=\#\{1\leq i\leq B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta):k_{i}\geq 1\}\leq k
.
Note that all products of binomial coefficients above are uniformly bounded by a constant depending only on
k
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}R_{n}(nt,\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda})\bigg|^{k}\,\Bigg|\,B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)\Bigg]\leq C\cdot(1+t)^{k}\cdot\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{k}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k-k_{1}}\cdots\sum_{k_{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}=0}^{k-\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)-1}k_{i}}\frac{\big(n(1-p_{n})\big)^{k-J}}{|1-p_{n}|^{k}}.

Using the multinomial distribution and then the combination formula to the last multiple sum,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}R_{n}(nt,\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda})\bigg|^{k}\,\Bigg|\,B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)\Bigg]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot(1+t)^{k}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{k}{{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}\choose{j}}\cdot\frac{\big(n(1-p_{n})\big)^{k-j}}{|1-p_{n}|^{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot(1+t)^{k}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{k}\big|B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)\big|^{j}\cdot\frac{\big(n(1-p_{n})\big)^{k-j}}{|1-p_{n}|^{k}}.

Taking expectations on both sides of this inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1}(t)\big|^{k}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot(1+t)^{k}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)\big|^{j}\Big]\cdot\big(n(1-p_{n})\big)^{-j}.

Notice that
n(1-p_{n})\to\bar{\bar{\nu}}(0)\in(0,\infty]
as
n\to\infty
, we have uniformly in
j\leq k
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)\big|^{j}\Big]=\sum_{i=1}^{j}{{j}\choose{i}}\big([n\zeta]\cdot(1-p_{n})\big)^{i}\leq C\cdot(\zeta\vee\zeta^{j})\cdot\Big(\big(n(1-p_{n})\big)\vee\big(n(1-p_{n})\big)^{j}\Big).

The second inequality in (
4.45
) follows by combining the preceding two inequality together.

\Box

We are now ready to establish the uniform upper bounds for all moments of the processes
\{X^{(n)}_{\zeta}\}_{n\geq 1}
with the help of the preceding results and the moment estimates for stochastic Volterra integrals given in Proposition
2.5
.

Lemma 4.19

For each
p\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
\zeta,t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(t)\big|^{p}\Big]\leq C\cdot(\zeta\vee\zeta^{p})\cdot(1+t)^{2p-1}.

(4.46)

Proof.
For
p=1
, taking expectations on both sides of (
4.17
), the non-negativity of
X_{\zeta}^{(n)}
and Proposition
4.18
induce that uniformly in
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(t)\big|\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\Big[\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1}(t)\Big]+\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\Big[\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2}(t)\Big]\leq C\cdot\zeta\cdot(1+t).

By induction, we now proceed under the assumption that (
4.46
) holds for some
p\geq 1
and prove that it also holds for
2p
.
By the power inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(t)\big|^{2p}\big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{i}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big],

(4.47)

for some constant
C>0
independent of
n
and
t
.
By Proposition
4.18
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]+\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot(\zeta\vee\zeta^{2p})\cdot(1+t)^{2p},

(4.48)

uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
\zeta,t\geq 0
.
Applying (
2.7
) to
\mathbf{E}\big[|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3}(t)|^{2p}\big]
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
p
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(r)\big|^{p}\Big]\cdot\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{|R_{n}\big(ns,y\big)|^{2}}{n^{2}}\cdot n^{2}\gamma_{n}(1-\theta_{n})\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,ds\,dy\bigg|^{p}

\displaystyle+C\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(r)\big|\Big]\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{|R_{n}\big(ns,y\big)|^{2p}}{n^{2p}}\cdot n^{2}\gamma_{n}(1-\theta_{n})\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,ds\,dy.

By (
4.46
), Corollary
4.15
as well as the two facts that
\gamma_{n}\leq 1/c
and
\theta_{n}\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot(\zeta\vee\zeta^{p})\cdot(1+t)^{p}+C\cdot\zeta\cdot(1+t)\leq C\cdot(\zeta\vee\zeta^{p})\cdot(1+t)^{p},

(4.49)

uniformly in
\zeta,t\geq 0
.
Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(r)\big|^{p}\Big]\cdot\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{|R_{n}\big(ns,y\big)|^{2}}{n^{2}}\cdot n^{2}\gamma_{n}\theta_{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,\cdot ds\cdot dy\bigg|^{p}

\displaystyle+C\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(r)\big|\Big]\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{|R_{n}\big(ns,y\big)|^{2p}}{n^{2p}}\cdot n^{2}\gamma_{n}\theta_{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,\cdot ds\cdot dy,

which also can be bounded by

C\cdot(\zeta\vee\zeta^{p})\cdot(1+t)^{4p-1}+C\cdot\zeta\cdot(1+t)^{2p+1}\leq C\cdot(\zeta\vee\zeta^{p})\cdot(1+t)^{4p-1},

uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
\zeta,t\geq 0
by using (
4.46
) and Corollary
4.15
again.
Taking this and (
4.48
)-(
4.49
) back into (
4.47
) induces that
the inequality (
4.46
) holds for
2p
.

\Box

4.3
C
-tightness

In the next four subsections, we will prove the
C
-tightness of four sequences
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{i}\}_{n\geq 1}
,
i=1,2,3,4,
,
separately, which together with Corollary 3.33 in
[
40
, p.353]
induces the
C
-tightness of the sequence

\big\{(X^{(n)}_{\zeta},\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1},\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2},\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3},\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4})\big\}_{n\geq 1}.

4.3.1
Convergence in probability of
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1}\}_{n\geq 1}

Note that
\mathbf{E}\big[\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_{n,i}^{c}>t\}}\big]=\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(t)
for
t\geq 0
.
In view of (
4.18
),
centralizing each summand in
\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1}
allows us to write it into

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1}(t)=\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(nt)+R_{\varPi_{n}}*\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(nt)+\frac{B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)}{n}\cdot\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,2}(nt),\quad t\geq 0,

(4.50)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(t):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_{n,i}^{c}>t\}}-\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(t)\big)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,2}(t):=\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(t)+R_{\varPi_{n}}*\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(t).

In the next two propositions, we prove the convergence in probability of the three terms on the right side of (
4.50
) separately.

Proposition 4.20

We have
\sup_{t\geq 0}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(t)\big|+\big\|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}\big\|_{L^{1}}+\sup_{t\geq 0}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}*\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(t)\big|\overset{\rm p}{\to}0
as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.
We first prove
\sup_{t\geq 0}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(t)\big|\to 0
in probability by using the Glivenko-Cantelli theorem.
Notice that random variables
\ell_{n,i}^{c}
,
i,n\geq 1
, are i.i.d., without loss of generality we may omit the subcript
n
and write
\{\ell_{n,i}^{c}\}_{i\geq 1}
into
\{\ell_{i}^{c}\}_{i\geq 1}
.
For each
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\geq 0}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(t)\big|\geq\epsilon\bigg)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\geq 0}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(t)\big|\geq\epsilon;B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)<[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}\bigg)

(4.52)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\geq 0}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(t)\big|\geq\epsilon;B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)\geq[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}\bigg).

By the Chernoff inequality for binomial random variables
1
1

1

For a binomial random variable
X
with parameter
(k,p)
, we have
\mathbf{P}(X\leq kp-x)\leq\exp\{-\frac{x^{2}}{2kp}\}
for any
x\geq 0
.
, the first probability on the right side of (
4.52
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)<[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}\Big)\leq\exp\Big\{-\frac{[n\zeta]^{4/3}}{2[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}}\Big\},

which goes to
0
as
n\to\infty
.
Further, conditioned on
B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)\geq[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(t)\big|\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_{i}^{c}>t\}}-\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(t)\big)\bigg|+\sup_{t\geq 0}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}}^{B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_{i}^{c}>t\}}-\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(t)\big)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_{i}^{c}>t\}}-\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(t)\big)\bigg|+\frac{2}{n}\cdot\Big|B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)-[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}\Big|+\frac{2\cdot[n\zeta]^{2/3}}{n}.

Hence for
n\geq 64\cdot\zeta^{2}/\epsilon^{3}
, the second probability on the right side of (
4.52
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_{i}^{c}>t\}}-\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(t)\big)\bigg|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Bigg)+\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\frac{2}{n}\cdot\Big|B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)-[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\Bigg)

By the Glivenko-Cantelli theorem and the fact that
[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}\sim\zeta\cdot n
, the first probability vanishes as
n\to\infty
.
By Chebyshev’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\frac{2}{n}\cdot\Big|B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)-[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}\Big|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\Bigg)\leq\frac{32}{\epsilon^{2}}\cdot\frac{{\rm Var}\big(B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)\big)}{n^{2}}=\frac{32}{\epsilon^{2}}\cdot\frac{[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}(1-p_{n})}{n^{2}}\to 0,

(4.53)

as
n\to\infty
.
Putting these estimates together and then taking them back into (
4.52
) induce that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\geq 0}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(t)\big|\geq\epsilon\bigg)=0.

We now prove
\big\|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}\big\|_{L^{1}}\to 0
in probability, which along with Lemma
4.4
induces that as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}*\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}(t)\big|\leq\frac{1}{c}\cdot\big\|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}\big\|_{L^{1}}\to 0.

Similarly as in the previous argument, for each
\epsilon>0
we also have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}\big\|_{L^{1}}\geq\epsilon\Big)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sim\!\!\!

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}\big\|_{L^{1}}\geq\epsilon;B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)\geq[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}\Big),

as
n\to\infty
.
It is easy to see that conditioned on
B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)\geq[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}
,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}\big\|_{L^{1}}\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_{i}^{c}>s\}}-\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(s)\big)\bigg|\,ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}+1}^{B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_{i}^{c}>s\}}-\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(s)\big)\bigg|\,ds.

Here the second integral on the right side can be uniformly bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}+1}^{[n\zeta]}\ell_{i}^{c}+c\cdot\frac{|B_{n}^{c}(\zeta)-[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}|}{n}+c\cdot\frac{[n\zeta]^{2/3}}{n}.

The last two terms vanish in probability as
n\to\infty
; see (
4.53
).
Moreover, an application of the law of large number to the first term induces that almost surely

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}+1}^{[n\zeta]}\ell_{i}^{c}\sim\frac{[n\zeta](1-p_{n})+[n\zeta]^{2/3}}{n}\cdot\mathbf{E}\big[\ell_{1}^{c}\big]\to 0,

as
n\to\infty
.
In conclusion, we can obtain that
\|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,1}\|_{L^{1}}\overset{\rm p}{\to}0
as
n\to\infty
if

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_{i}^{c}>s\}}-\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(s)\big)\bigg|\,ds\overset{\rm p}{\to}0.

(4.54)

To prove this convergence in probability, we define a sequence of i.i.d.
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
-valued random variables
\{X_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
with

\displaystyle X_{i}(t):=\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_{i}^{c}>t\}}-\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(t),\quad t\geq 0.

Note that
\mathbf{E}\big[\|X_{i}\|_{L^{1}}\big]\leq 2/c
and
\mathbf{E}\big[X_{i}(t)\big]=0
for any
t\geq 0
.
The left-hand side of (
4.54
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\zeta\cdot\bigg\|\frac{1}{[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}}\sum_{i=1}^{[n\zeta]\cdot p_{n}-[n\zeta]^{2/3}}X_{i}\bigg\|_{L^{1}},

which goes to
0
in probability as
n\to\infty
by Corollary 7.10
[
55
, p.189]
.
The whole proof ends.

\Box

Proposition 4.21

For each
T\geq 0
, we have
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{c,2}(nt)-c\cdot W^{\prime}(t)\big|\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.
For some constant
\beta>0
, multiplying both sides of (
4.21
) by the function
\frac{1}{\gamma_{n}}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}\cdot t}
and then integrating them over
(t,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma_{n}}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}R_{\varPi_{n}}(x)dx\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(x)dx+\int_{t}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{x}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(x-y)}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(x-y)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}R_{\varPi_{n}}(y)dydx.\quad

(4.55)

Note that the last double integral equals to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{x}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(x-y)}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(x-y)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}R_{\varPi_{n}}(y)dydx-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{x}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(x-y)}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(x-y)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}R_{\varPi_{n}}(y)dydx.

Applying Fubini’s theorem to these two double integrals, they equal to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}R_{\varPi_{n}}(y)dy\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(x)dx-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(t-x)}R_{\varPi_{n}}(t-x)\int_{0}^{x}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(y)dydx

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}R_{\varPi_{n}}(y)dy\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(x)dx+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(t-x)}R_{\varPi_{n}}(t-x)\int_{x}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(y)dydx.

Plugging this back into the right side of (
4.55
) and then making some simple transform,

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(x)\,dx+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(t-x)}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(t-x)\int_{x}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}R_{\varPi_{n}}(y)\,dydx

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma_{n}}\bigg(1-\gamma_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}z}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(z)\,dz\bigg)\cdot\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}R_{\varPi_{n}}(x)\,dx,

which along with the second equality in (
4.6
) induces that

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(x)dx+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(t-y)}R_{\varPi_{n}}(t-y)\int_{y}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(x)dxdy

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma_{n}}\Big(1-\int_{0}^{\infty}\gamma_{n}\cdot e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(x)dx\Big)\cdot\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}R_{\varPi_{n}}(x)dx

\displaystyle-\theta_{n}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(x)dx-\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(t-y)}R_{\varPi_{n}}(t-y)\int_{y}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(x)dxdy.

Since
\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}\cdot x}\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(x)dx=(\beta/n+1/c)^{-1}e^{-(\beta/n+1/c)t}
, the left-hand side of this equality equals to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta/n+1/c}\Big(e^{-(\frac{\beta}{n}+\frac{1}{c})t}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(t-y)}R_{\varPi_{n}}(t-y)e^{-(\frac{\beta}{n}+\frac{1}{c})y}\,dy\Big)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}t}}{\beta/n+1/c}\big(\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(t)+R_{\varPi_{n}}*\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(t)\big).

These two equations together with the fact that
c\gamma_{n}\sim 1
induce that

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,2}(nt)=\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(nt)+R_{\varPi_{n}}*\overline{\boldsymbol{E}}_{c}(nt)\sim e^{\beta t}\cdot\Big(I_{1}^{(n)}(\beta,t)-I_{2}^{(n)}(\beta,t)-I_{3}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\Big),

(4.56)

locally uniformly in
t,\beta\geq 0
as
n\to\infty
with

\displaystyle I_{1}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\Big(1-\gamma_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}z}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(z)dz\Big)\cdot\int_{nt}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}R_{\varPi_{n}}(x)\,dx,

\displaystyle I_{2}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{nt}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(y)\,dy,

\displaystyle I_{3}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{0}^{nt}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(nt-x)}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt-x)\int_{x}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(y)\,dydx.

By using the change of variables and then (
4.26
) with
\lambda=\beta
and Lemma
4.5
,

\displaystyle I_{1}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle n\bigg(1-\gamma_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}z}\overline{\varPi}_{n}(z)dz\bigg)\cdot\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta x}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nx)dx-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta x}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nx)dx\bigg)

(4.57)

\displaystyle\to\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\varPhi(\beta)}{\beta}\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta x}W^{\prime}(x)dx-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta x}W^{\prime}(x)dx\Big)

(4.58)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\varPhi(\beta)}{\beta}\cdot\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta x}W^{\prime}(x)dx,

(4.59)

uniformly in
t
and
\beta
on compacts as
n\to\infty
.
Additionally, by Proposition
4.1
we also have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\beta\geq 0}\sup_{t\geq 0}I_{2}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{0}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(x)\,dx=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dy=0.

(4.60)

For
I_{3}^{(n)}(\beta,t)
, we can split it into the following two terms

\displaystyle I_{3,1}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{0}^{nt}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(nt-x)}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt-x)dx\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(y)dy,

(4.61)

\displaystyle I_{3,2}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle-\frac{\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{0}^{nt}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}(nt-x)}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt-x)\int_{0}^{x}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(y)dydx.

(4.62)

By Fubini’s theorem and the change of variables,

\displaystyle I_{3,1}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{0}^{nt}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}x}R_{\varPi_{n}}(x)\,dx\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}y}\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(y)\,dy\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}z}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(z)\,dz

(4.63)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{n\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta x}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nx)\,dx\cdot\frac{n}{\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n})}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dy\cdot\frac{n/c}{n/c+\beta}

(4.64)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{n/c}{n/c+\beta}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta(t-x)}R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t-x)\big)\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dydx.

(4.65)

For
I_{3,2}^{(n)}(\beta,t)
, by using Fubini’s theorem again we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}\cdot y}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(y)dy\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}\cdot y}\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(y)\int_{0}^{x-y}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}\cdot z}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(z)dzdy

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}\frac{n/c}{n/c+\beta}\big(1-e^{-(\beta/n+1/c)\cdot(x-y)}\big)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}\cdot y}\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(y)dy.

Plugging this back into the right side of (
4.62
) gives that

\displaystyle I_{3,2}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle-\frac{\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{0}^{nt}e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}\cdot(nt-x)}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nt-x)\int_{0}^{x}\frac{n/c}{n/c+\beta}\big(1-e^{-(\beta/n+1/c)\cdot(x-y)}\big)e^{-\frac{\beta}{n}\cdot y}\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(y)\,dydx

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle-\frac{n^{2}\theta_{n}}{c}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta(t-x)}R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t-x)\big)\int_{0}^{x}\frac{n/c}{n/c+\beta}\big(1-e^{-(\beta+n/c)\cdot(x-y)}\big)e^{-\beta y}\overline{\Lambda}_{n}(ny)dydx

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle-\frac{n/c}{n/c+\beta}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta(t-x)}R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t-x)\big)\int_{0}^{x}e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dydx

\displaystyle+\frac{n/c}{n/c+\beta}e^{-\beta t}\int_{0}^{t}R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t-x)\big)\int_{0}^{x}e^{-\frac{n}{c}\cdot(x-y)}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dydx.

Combing this together with (
4.63
), we have as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle I_{3}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sim\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta(t-x)}R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t-x)\big)\int_{x}^{\infty}e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dydx

(4.67)

\displaystyle+e^{-\beta t}\int_{0}^{t}R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t-x)\big)\int_{0}^{x}e^{-\frac{n}{c}(x-y)}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dydx,

locally uniformly in
t,\beta\geq 0
.
Applying Fubini’s theorem to the second double integral on the right side and then using Lemma
4.4
, it equals to

\displaystyle e^{-\beta t}\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+x)\,dx\int_{0}^{t-x}R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t-x-y)\big)e^{-\frac{n}{c}y}\,dy\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c}\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+x)\,dx\cdot\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{n}{c}\cdot y}\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+x)\,dx,

which vanishes uniformly in
t,\beta\geq 0
as
n\to\infty
; see Proposition
4.1
.
For the first term on the right side of (
4.67
), an application of Fubini’s theorem again induces that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta(t-x)}R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(t-x)\big)\int_{x}^{\infty}e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dydx=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\int^{t}_{t-y}e^{-\beta x}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nx)\,dxdy,

which can be written as the sum of the following two terms

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{1}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int^{t}_{t-y}e^{-\beta z}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nz)\,dz-\int^{t}_{t-y}e^{-\beta z}W^{\prime}(z)\,dz\Big)e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dy,

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{2}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int^{t}_{t-y}e^{-\beta z}W^{\prime}(z)\,dz\Big)\cdot e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dy.

By using the dominated convergence theorem along with (
1.2
) and the fact that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int^{t}_{t-y}e^{-\beta z}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nz)\,dz\Big|+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int^{t}_{t-y}e^{-\beta z}W^{\prime}(z)\,dz\Big|\leq C\cdot(T\wedge y),

uniformly in
T,y,\beta\geq 0
; see Lemma
4.4
and (
1.7
),
and then using Lemma
4.5
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big|A^{(n)}_{1}(\beta,t)\big|\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int^{t}_{t-y}e^{-\beta z}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nz)\,dz-\int^{t}_{t-y}e^{-\beta z}W^{\prime}(z)dz\Big|e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy=0.

Moreover, applying the monotone convergence theorem to
A^{(n)}_{2}(\beta,t)
we also have

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{2}(\beta,t)\to\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int^{t}_{t-y}e^{-\beta z}W^{\prime}(z)\,dz\Big)\cdot e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy,

uniformly in
\beta
and
t
on compacts as
n\to\infty
.
Taking all these limits back into (
4.67
),

\displaystyle I_{3}^{(n)}(\beta,t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\to\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int^{t}_{t-y}e^{-\beta z}W^{\prime}(z)dz\Big)\cdot e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(y)dy,

locally uniformly in
t
and
\beta
as
n\to\infty
.
Taking this and (
4.57
)-(
4.60
) back into (
4.56
) induces that

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,2}(nt)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\to\!\!\!

\displaystyle e^{\beta t}\cdot\Big(1-\frac{\varPhi(\beta)}{\beta}\cdot\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\beta x}W^{\prime}(x)\,dx-\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int^{t}_{t-y}e^{-\beta z}W^{\prime}(z)\,dz\Big)\cdot e^{-\beta y}\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy\Big),

uniformly in
t
and
\beta
on compacts.
Note that
\varPhi(\beta)/\beta\to b
as
\beta\to 0+
.
Finally, by (
1.12
) and (
1.14
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1,2}(nt)\overset{\rm u.c.}{\to}1-bW(t)-\int_{0}^{\infty}\int^{t}_{t-y}W^{\prime}(z)\,dz\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy=c\cdot W^{\prime}(t),

as
n\to\infty
and the whole proof ends.

\Box

The next lemma follows immediately by applying the locally uniform convergence results in the preceding two propositions and Lemma
4.4
to the right side of (
4.50
).

Lemma 4.22

For each
T\geq 0
, we have
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1}(t)-\zeta\cdot c\cdot W^{\prime}(t)\big|\overset{\rm p}{\to}0
as
n\to\infty
.

4.3.2
Weak convergence of
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2}\}_{n\geq 1}

By (
4.22
) and the change of variables, we can split
\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2}(t)
into the following two terms

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,1}(t):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}\geq nt\}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}(t):=\sum_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}\int_{t-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}}^{t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds.

The next proposition is a direct consequence of the fact that
B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)/n\sim\zeta\cdot p_{n}\overset{\rm p}{\to}0
as
n\to\infty
.

Proposition 4.23

We have
\sup_{t\geq 0}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,1}(t)\big|\overset{\rm p}{\to}0
as
n\to\infty
.

Proposition 4.24

The sequence
\big\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}\big\}_{n\geq 1}
is
C
-tight.

Proof.
By Aldous’s tightness criterion; see Theorem 1 in
[
3
]
, it suffices to prove that for each
T\geq 0
and
\varepsilon>0
, there exist constants
n_{0}\geq 1
and
\delta_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq n_{0}}\sup_{0<\delta\leq\delta_{0}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{S}_{T}^{(n)}}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}(\tau+\delta)-\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}(\tau)\big|\Big]\leq\varepsilon,

(4.68)

where
\mathcal{S}_{T}^{(n)}
is the collection of all
(\mathscr{F}_{nt})
-stopping times bounded by
T
.
We first have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}(\tau+\delta)-\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}(\tau)\Big|\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}\bigg(\int_{\tau+\delta-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}}^{\tau+\delta}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)ds-\int_{\tau-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}}^{\tau}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)ds\bigg)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}\bigg|\int_{\tau+\delta-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}}^{\tau+\delta}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)ds-\int_{\tau-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}}^{\tau}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)ds\bigg|.

When
\delta\geq\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}/n
, the disjunction of the two intervals
(\tau+\delta-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n},\tau+\delta]
and
(\tau-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n},\tau]
along with Lemma
4.4
induces that

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\tau+\delta-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}}^{\tau+\delta}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)ds-\int_{\tau-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}}^{\tau}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)ds\bigg|\leq\frac{2}{c}\cdot\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}.

(4.69)

When
\delta<\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}/n
, we also have
(\tau+\delta-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n},\tau+\delta]\cap(\tau-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n},\tau]=(\tau+\delta-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n},\tau]
and then

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\tau+\delta-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}}^{\tau+\delta}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)ds-\int_{\tau-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}}^{\tau}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)ds\bigg|

(4.70)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\tau}^{\tau+\delta}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)ds+\int_{\tau-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}}^{\tau-\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}+\delta}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)ds\bigg|\leq\frac{2}{c}\cdot\delta.

(4.71)

In conclusion, we have uniformly in
n\geq 1
,
\tau\in\mathcal{S}_{T}^{(n)}
and
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}(\tau+\delta)-\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}(\tau)\Big|\leq\frac{2}{c}\sum_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}\Big(\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}\wedge\delta\Big).

(4.72)

By using the independence between
B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)
and
\{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}\}_{j\geq 1}
and then Proposition
4.16
with
p=1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in\mathcal{S}_{T}^{(n)}}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}(\tau+\delta)-\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}(\tau)\big|\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{2}{c}\cdot\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\sum_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}\Big(\frac{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}}{n}\wedge\delta\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{2}{c}\cdot\mathbf{E}\big[B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)\big]\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\frac{\ell_{n,1}^{\Lambda}}{n}\wedge\delta\Big]

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{2}{c}\cdot[n\zeta]\cdot(1-p_{n})\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\frac{\ell_{n,1}^{\Lambda}}{n}\wedge\delta\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\Big(1-p_{n}+\int_{0}^{\infty}(y\wedge\delta)\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy\Big),

which goes to
0
as
n\to\infty
and
\delta\to 0+
. Hence (
4.68
) holds.

\Box

Lemma 4.25

As
n\to\infty
, we have that
\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2}
converges weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})
to

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{2}(t):=\int_{0}^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(t)-W(t-y)\big)N_{0}(dz,dy),\quad t\geq 0.

Proof.
By Proposition
4.23
and
4.24
, the sequence
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2}\}_{n\geq 1}
is
C
-tight and it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{2,2}^{(n)}(t_{i})\bigg\}\Bigg]=\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{2}(t_{i})\bigg\}\Bigg]

(4.73)

for any
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}<\cdots<t_{d}
and
\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{d}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
By the exponential formula of stochastic integral with respect to Poisson random measure; see
[
14
, p.8]
, we first have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{2}(t_{i})\bigg\}\Bigg]

(4.74)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\exp\bigg\{-\int_{0}^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\big(W(t_{i})-W(t_{i}-y)\big)N_{0}(dz,dy)\bigg\}\Bigg]

(4.75)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\exp\Bigg\{-\zeta\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(1-\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\big(W(t_{i})-W(t_{i}-y)\big)\bigg\}\bigg)\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy\Bigg\}.

(4.76)

On the other hand, by the mutual-independence among
B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)
and
\{\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}\}_{j\geq 1}
and then the Laplace transform of binomial distribution,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\cdot\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}(t_{i})\bigg\}\Bigg]\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{j=1}^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\int_{t_{i}-\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}/n}^{t_{i}}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{E}\bigg[\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\int_{t_{i}-\ell_{n,1}^{\Lambda}/n}^{t_{i}}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\bigg\}\bigg]^{B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\Bigg[p_{n}+\big(1-p_{n}\big)\cdot\mathbf{E}\bigg[\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\int_{t_{i}-\ell_{n,1}^{\Lambda}/n}^{t_{i}}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\bigg\}\bigg]\Bigg]^{[n\zeta]}

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\Bigg[1+\big(1-p_{n}\big)\cdot\mathbf{E}\bigg[\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\int_{t_{i}-\ell_{n,1}^{\Lambda}/n}^{t_{i}}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\bigg\}-1\bigg]\Bigg]^{[n\zeta]},

which is asymptotically equivalent to

\displaystyle\exp\Bigg\{-[n\zeta]\cdot\big(1-p_{n}\big)\cdot\mathbf{E}\bigg[1-\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\int_{t_{i}-\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}/n}^{t_{i}}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\bigg\}\bigg]\Bigg\},

since both
1-p_{n}
and the last expectation go to
0
as
n\to\infty
.
By the definition of
\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}
and then using Fubini’s theorem as well as the change of variables,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\bigg[1-\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\int_{t_{i}-\ell_{n,j}^{\Lambda}/n}^{t_{i}}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(1-\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\int_{t_{i}-x/n}^{t_{i}}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\bigg\}\bigg)\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*}_{n}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(x)\,dx\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(1-\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\int_{t_{i}-(y+x)/n}^{t_{i}}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\bigg\}\bigg)\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*}_{n}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(x)\,dx\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(1-\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\int_{t_{i}-(y+x)/n}^{t_{i}}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\bigg\}\bigg)\frac{\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y/n)}{n\int_{\eta_{n}}^{\infty}\bar{\nu}(z)dz}\,dy

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(x)\,dx\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(1-\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\int_{t_{i}-y-x/n}^{t_{i}}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\bigg\}\bigg)\frac{\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)}{\int_{\eta_{n}}^{\infty}\bar{\nu}(z)dz}\,dy.

Combing this together with the fact that
[n\zeta]\cdot\big(1-p_{n}\big)\sim\zeta\cdot\int_{\eta_{n}}\bar{\nu}(z)dz
; see (
4.15
), we have as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\cdot\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2,2}(t_{i})\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\sim\!\!\!

\displaystyle\exp\Bigg\{-\zeta\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(x)\,dx\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(1-\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\int_{t_{i}-y-x/n}^{t_{i}}R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\,ds\bigg\}\bigg)\bar{\nu}(\eta_{n}+y)\,dy\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\to\!\!\!

\displaystyle\exp\Bigg\{-\zeta\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(1-\exp\bigg\{-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}\big(W(t_{i})-W(t_{i}-y)\big)\bigg\}\bigg)\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy\Bigg\},

which together with (
4.74
) immediately induces (
4.73
).

\Box

4.3.3
C
-tightness of
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3}\}_{n\geq 1}

In this section we prove the
C
-tightness of the sequence
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3}\}_{n\geq 1}
.
Plugging (
4.22
) into (
4.19
), we can split
\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3}(t)
into the following two terms.

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{y\geq n(t-s)\}}\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy),

(4.77)

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,2}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\cdot\Big(\int_{n(t-s)-y}^{n(t-s)}R_{\varPi_{n}}(r)dr\Big)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy).

(4.78)

By Corollary 3.33 in
[
40
, p.353]
, it suffices to prove the
C
-tightness of the two sequences
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,2}\}_{n\geq 1}
separately.
We first prove the weak convergence of the first sequence to
0
by using the following
C
-tightness criterion for càdlàg stochastic processes established in
[
38
]
.

Proposition 4.26

For a sequence of càdlàg stochastic processes
\{X^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
defined on a common probability space with
\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\big[|X^{(n)}(0)|^{q}\big]<\infty
for some
q>0
, it is
C
-tight if the following two conditions hold for any
T\geq 0
and some constant
\beta>2
.

(1)

There exist some constants
C>0
,
p\geq 1
and
\rho,\alpha>0
such that for any
\delta\in(0,1)
and
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X^{(n)}(t+\delta)-X^{(n)}(t)\big|^{p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\Big(\delta^{1+\rho}+\frac{\delta}{n^{\alpha}}\Big).

(2)

\displaystyle\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[Tn^{\beta}]}\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\beta}]}\big|X^{(n)}(k/n^{\beta}+\delta)-X^{(n)}(k/n^{\beta})\big|\overset{\rm p}{\to}0
as
n\to\infty
.

Lemma 4.27

We have
\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}\to 0
weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.
For each
t\geq 0
, by using (
2.7
) along with Lemma
4.19
as well as the two facts that
c\gamma_{n}\leq 1
and
\theta_{n}\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}(t)\big|^{2}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{y\geq n(t-s)\}}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dsdy\leq C\cdot\int_{0}^{t}e^{-ns/c}ds\leq\frac{C}{n},

which goes to
0
as
n\to\infty
. This induces that

\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}\overset{\rm f.f.d.}{\longrightarrow}0.

For the tightness, it suffices to prove that
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}\}_{n\geq 1}
satisfies the two conditions in Proposition
4.26
.
For each
t\geq 0
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}(t+\delta)-\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}(t)=J^{(n)}_{1,1}(t,\delta)-J^{(n)}_{1,2}(t,\delta)

(4.79)

with

\displaystyle J^{(n)}_{1,1}(t,\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{n(t+\delta-s)}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy),

\displaystyle J^{(n)}_{1,2}(t,\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{n(t-s)}^{n(t+\delta-s)}\frac{1}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy).

Condition (1).

For any
p\geq 1
, by using (
2.7
) along with Lemma
4.19
and then the change of variables, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
n
and
\delta
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J^{(n)}_{1,1}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X^{(n)}_{\zeta}(t)\big|^{p}\Big]\cdot\Big|\int_{0}^{\delta}ds\int_{ns}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\Big|^{p}

(4.81)

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{n^{2p-2}}\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X^{(n)}_{\zeta}(t)\big|\Big]\cdot\int_{0}^{\delta}ds\int_{ns}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\Big(\delta^{p}+\frac{\delta}{n^{2p-2}}\Big).

(4.82)

Similarly, we also have uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J^{(n)}_{1,2}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X^{(n)}_{\zeta}(t)\big|^{p}\Big]\cdot\Big|\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{ns}^{n(s+\delta)}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\Big|^{p}

(4.84)

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{n^{2p-2}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X^{(n)}_{\zeta}(t)\big|^{p}\Big]\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{ns}^{n(s+\delta)}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\Big|\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{ns}^{n(s+\delta)}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\Big|^{p}+\frac{C}{n^{2p-2}}\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{ns}^{n(s+\delta)}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy.

(4.85)

A simple calculation along with the inequality that
x(1-e^{-1/x})\leq 1
for any
x\geq 0
shows

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{ns}^{n(s+\delta)}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}e^{-\frac{ns}{c}}\,ds\cdot\big(1-e^{-n\delta/c}\big)\leq\frac{c}{n}\cdot\big(1-e^{-n\delta/c}\big)\leq\delta.

Plugging this back into (
4.84
) gives that uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J^{(n)}_{1,2}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\Big(\delta^{p}+\frac{\delta}{n^{2p-2}}\Big).

(4.86)

Armed with the two upper-bound estimates (
4.81
) and (
4.86
), we use the power inequality to get

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}(t+\delta)-\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J^{(n)}_{1,1}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]

\displaystyle+C\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J^{(n)}_{1,2}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\Big(\delta^{p}+\frac{\delta}{n^{2p-2}}\Big),

uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
and hence Condition (1) in Proposition
4.26
is satisfied.

Condition (2).
For
\beta>2
and each
T>0
, by (
4.79
) it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\beta}]}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}(k/n^{\beta}+\delta)-\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,1}(k/n^{\beta})\big|

(4.87)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\beta}]}\big|J^{(n)}_{1,1}(k/n^{\beta},\delta)\big|+\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\beta}]}\big|J^{(n)}_{1,2}(k/n^{\beta},\delta)\big|.

(4.88)

It suffices to prove that both of the two terms on the right side converge in probability to
0
.

(i)
By the facts that
c\gamma_{n}\leq 1
and
\theta_{n}\in(0,1)
, we have uniformly in
n\geq 1
,
k\geq 1
and
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\beta}]}\big|J^{(n)}_{1,1}(k/n^{\beta},\delta)\big|

(4.89)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\beta}]}\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{k/n^{\beta}+\delta}\frac{n}{c}\cdot X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)\,ds\int_{n(t+\delta-s)}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy

(4.91)

\displaystyle+\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\beta}]}\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{k/n^{\beta}+\delta}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{n(t+\delta-s)}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,N^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\frac{n}{c}\cdot X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)\,ds+\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,N^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy).

(4.92)

By the definition of compensated Poisson random measure, the last stochastic integral on the right side of the second inequality can be bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\frac{n}{c}\cdot X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)\,ds+\Bigg|\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)\Bigg|.

Therefore, we have uniformly in
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\beta}]}\big|J^{(n)}_{c,1}(k/n^{\beta},\delta)\big|

(4.93)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\frac{2n}{c}\cdot X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)\,ds

(4.95)

\displaystyle+\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\Bigg|\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)\Bigg|.

For any
\epsilon>0
, by using Chebyshev’s inequality, Hölder’s inequality and then Lemma
4.19
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\frac{2n}{c}\cdot X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)\,ds\geq\epsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{[n^{\beta}T]}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\frac{2n}{c}\cdot X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)\,ds\geq\epsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2p}}\sum_{k=0}^{[n^{\beta}T]}\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\frac{2n}{c}\cdot X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)\,ds\bigg|^{2p}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2p}}\cdot n^{2p-(2p-1)\beta}\sum_{k=0}^{[n^{\beta}T]}\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)\big|^{2p}\Big]\,ds,

which can be bounded by
C\cdot n^{2p-(2p-1)\beta}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
for all
p\geq 2
.
Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)\geq\epsilon\bigg)

(4.96)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{[n^{\beta}T]}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)\geq\epsilon\bigg)

(4.97)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2p}}\sum_{k=0}^{[n^{\beta}T]}\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)\bigg|^{2p}\Bigg].

(4.98)

Similarly as in (
4.81
), the last expectation can be bounded by
C\cdot\big(n^{-p\beta}+n^{2-2p-\beta}\big)
uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
k\geq 0
.
Consequently, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\int_{k/n^{\beta}}^{(k+1)/n^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)\geq\epsilon\bigg)\leq C\cdot\big(n^{\beta(1-p)}+n^{2(1-p)}\big),

(4.99)

which also goes to
0
as
n\to\infty
for any
p>1
.
Taking the preceding two limits back into (
4.93
), we have as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\beta}]}\big|J^{(n)}_{1,1}(k/n^{\beta},\delta)\big|\overset{\rm p}{\to}0.

(ii)
We now prove that the second term on the right-hand side of (
4.87
) converges in probability to
0
.
Similarly as in (
4.89
), we also have

\displaystyle\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\beta}]}\big|J^{(n)}_{1,2}(k/n^{\beta},\delta)\big|\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{k/n^{\beta}}X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)ds\int_{n(k/n^{\beta}-s)}^{n((k+1)/n^{\beta}-s)}\frac{2n}{c}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle+\Bigg|\int_{0}^{k/n^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{n(k/n^{\beta}-s)}^{n((k+1)/n^{\beta}-s)}\frac{1}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)\Bigg|,

and then

\displaystyle\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\beta}]}\big|J^{(n)}_{1,2}(k/n^{\beta},\delta)\big|

(4.100)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\int_{0}^{k/n^{\beta}}X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)ds\int_{n(k/n^{\beta}-s)}^{n((k+1)/n^{\beta}-s)}\frac{2n}{c}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy

(4.102)

\displaystyle+\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\bigg|\int_{0}^{k/n^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{n(k/n^{\beta}-s)}^{n((k+1)/n^{\beta}-s)}\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)\bigg|.\qquad

As before, it suffices to prove the convergence in probability of the two terms on the right-hand side to
0
.
Firstly, taking expectation of the first term and then using the next inequality

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0}\sup_{s\geq 0}\int_{n(k/n^{\beta}-s)}^{n((k+1)/n^{\beta}-s)}\frac{2n}{c}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\leq\frac{2n^{2-\beta}}{c^{2}},

uniformly in
n\geq 1
and Lemma
4.19
, we can get that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\int_{0}^{k/n^{\beta}}X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)ds\int_{n(k/n^{\beta}-s)}^{n((k+1)/n^{\beta}-s)}\frac{2n}{c}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\Bigg]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n^{\beta-2}}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{E}\big[X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)\big]ds\leq\frac{C}{n^{\beta-2}},

which vanishes as
n\to\infty
since
\beta>2
.
For the second term, similarly as in (
4.96
) and (
4.99
) we also have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\beta}T]}\Bigg|\int_{0}^{k/n^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{n(k/n^{\beta}-s)}^{n((k+1)/n^{\beta}-s)}\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)\Bigg|\geq\epsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2p}}\sum_{k=0}^{[n^{\beta}T]}\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\int_{0}^{k/n^{\beta}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{n(k/n^{\beta}-s)}^{n((k+1)/n^{\beta}-s)}\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)\bigg|^{2p}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\epsilon^{2p}}\sum_{k=0}^{[n^{\beta}T]}\Bigg(\bigg|\int_{0}^{k/n^{\beta}}ds\int_{n(k/n^{\beta}-s)}^{n((k+1)/n^{\beta}-s)}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\bigg|^{p}+\int_{0}^{k/n^{\beta}}ds\int_{n(k/n^{\beta}-s)}^{n((k+1)/n^{\beta}-s)}\frac{\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)}{n^{2p-2}}\,dy\Bigg),

which can be bounded by
C\cdot\big(n^{p(1-\beta)+\beta}+n^{3-2p}\big)\to 0
as
n\to\infty
for all
p>2
.
Taking the preceding two limits back into (
4.100
), we have as
n\to\infty
