,

\displaystyle\sup_{k=0,1,\cdots,[n^{\theta}T]}\sup_{\delta\in[0,1/n^{\theta}]}\big|J^{(n)}_{1,2}(k/n^{\theta},\delta)\big|\overset{\rm p}{\to}0.

\Box

We now prove the
C
-tightness of the continuous processes
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,2}\}_{n\geq 1}
by using the well-known Kolmogorov-Chentsov tightness criterion for continuous processes; see Problem 4.11 in
[
42
, p.64]
.
The Kolmogorov-Chentsov tightness criterion states that a sequence of continuous processes
\{X^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
is tight if for each
T\geq 0
, there exist constants
C,\beta,p,\rho>0
such that for any
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X^{(n)}(0)\big|^{\beta}\Big]<\infty\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X^{(n)}(t+\delta)-X^{(n)}(t)\big|^{p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\delta^{1+\rho}.

(4.103)

Lemma 4.28

The sequence
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,2}\}_{n\geq 1}
is
C
-tight.

Proof.
It suffices to prove that the two inequalities in (
4.103
) hold for
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,2}\}_{n\geq 1}
.
The first one is obvious because
\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,2}(0)\overset{\rm a.s.}{=}0
for all
n\geq 1
. We now prove the second one.
For each
t\geq 0
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, by the change of variables we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,2}(t+\delta)-\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,2}(t)=J^{(n)}_{2,1}(t,\delta)+J^{(n)}_{2,2}(t,\delta),

where the two terms on the right side are given by

\displaystyle J^{(n)}_{2,1}(t,\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\cdot\Big(\int_{n(t+\delta-s)-y}^{n(t+\delta-s)}R_{\varPi_{n}}(r)dr\Big)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy),

\displaystyle J^{(n)}_{2,2}(t,\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\cdot\Big(\int_{n(t-s)-y}^{n(t-s)}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}(r+n\delta)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(r)\big)dr\Big)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy).

For any
p\geq 2
, by the power inequality we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,2}(t+\delta)-\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,2}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J^{(n)}_{2,1}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]+C\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J^{(n)}_{2,2}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big],

(4.104)

for some constant
C>0
relying only on
p
.
By using (
2.7
) along with Lemma
4.19
as well as the two facts that
c\gamma_{n}\leq 1
and
\theta_{n}\in(0,1)
, and then using the change of variables,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J^{(n)}_{2,1}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\Big(\big|A^{(n)}_{2}(\delta)\big|^{p}+A^{(n)}_{2p}(\delta)\Big),

(4.105)

for some constant
C>0
independent of
n
and
\delta
, where

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{2k}(\delta):=n^{2}\int_{0}^{\delta}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)dr\Big)^{2k}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy,\quad k\geq 1.

By the non-negativity and uniform boundedness of
R_{\varPi_{n}}
; see Lemma
4.4
,

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{2k}(\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\sup_{y\geq 0}\Big(\int_{t-y/n}^{t}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)dr\Big)^{2k-2}\int_{0}^{\delta}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}n^{2}\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)dr\Big)^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\delta}{c}\Big)^{2k-2}\cdot\int_{0}^{\delta}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{y}{c}\Big)^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy,

which can be bounded by
C\cdot\delta^{2k-1}
uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
\delta>0
.
Plugging this result with
k=1
and
p
back into (
4.105
), there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J^{(n)}_{2,1}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\big(\delta^{p}+\delta^{2p-1}\big).

(4.106)

For
J^{(n)}_{2,2}(t,\delta)
, similarly as in (
4.105
) we also have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J^{(n)}_{2,2}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\Big(\big|B^{(n)}_{2}(\delta)\big|^{p}+B^{(n)}_{2p}(\delta)\Big),

(4.107)

uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, where

\displaystyle B^{(n)}_{2k}(\delta):=n^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)dr\Big)^{2k}\,ds,\quad k\geq 1.

(4.108)

We first consider the inner integrals in
B^{(n)}_{2k}(\delta)
.
By using Hölder’s inequality and Corollary
4.13
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)dr\Big)^{2k}\,ds

(4.109)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\frac{y}{n}\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)^{2}dr\Big)^{k}\,ds

(4.110)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{y\geq 0}\Big(\int_{t-y/n}^{t}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)^{2}dr\Big)^{k-1}

(4.112)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{k}\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)^{2}dr\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\Big(\int_{-\infty}^{T}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)^{2}dr\Big)^{k-1}

(4.114)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{k}\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)^{2}dr\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\delta^{k-1}\cdot\Big(\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{k}\cdot\int_{-\infty}^{T}\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)^{2}dr\,ds,

(4.115)

uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
.
By the fact that
R_{\varPi_{n}}(t)=0
for any
t<0
and then using Fubini’s theorem to the last double integral,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T}\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)^{2}dr\,ds\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{T}\int_{(s-y/n)\vee(-\delta)}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)^{2}dr\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{-\delta}^{T}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(s+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\big|^{2}\,ds\int_{s}^{s+y/n}\,dr

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{y}{n}\cdot\int_{-\infty}^{T}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(s+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\big|^{2}\,ds,

which can be bounded by
C\cdot\frac{y}{n}\cdot\delta
uniformly in
n\geq 1
,
y>0
and
\delta\in(0,1)
.
Plugging this into the last term in (
4.109
) and then taking it back into (
4.108
), there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
n\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle B^{(n)}_{2k}(\delta)\leq C\cdot n^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{k+1}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\cdot\delta^{k}\leq\frac{C}{n^{k-1}}\cdot\delta^{k},

Taking this result with
k=1
and
p
back into (
4.107
) yields that uniformly in
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J^{(n)}_{2,2}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\delta^{p}.

Finally, plugging this and (
4.106
) back into (
4.104
) shows that the sequence
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3,2}\}_{n\geq 1}
satisfies the second inequality in (
4.103
) and hence is
C
-tight.

\Box

Corollary 4.29

The sequence
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3}\}_{n\geq 1}
is
C
-tight.

4.3.4
C
-tightness of
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4}\}_{n\geq 1}

In this section, we prove the
C
-tightness of the sequence
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4}\}_{n\geq 1}
.
By (
4.22
) and the change of variables, we can split the process
\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4}
into the following two terms

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,1}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{y\geq n(t-s)\}}\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy),

(4.116)

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,2}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{t-s-y/n}^{t-s}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)dr\Big)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy).

(4.117)

Lemma 4.30

For each
T\geq 0
, we have
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,1}(t)\big|\overset{\rm p}{\to}0
as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.
By the fact that
\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy)=N^{(n)}_{\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy)-\gamma_{n}\cdot n^{2}\theta_{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,ds\,dz\,dy
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,1}(t)\big|\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\cdot N^{(n)}_{\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy)+\gamma_{n}\cdot n\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{T}X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s)\,ds.

Taking expectations on both sides of this inequality and then using Lemma
4.19
as well as the fact that
c\gamma_{n}\leq 1
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,1}(t)\big|\bigg]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot n\theta_{n},

which goes to
0
as
n\to\infty
.
The desired locally uniform convergence in probability holds.

\Box

Lemma 4.31

The sequence
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,2}\}_{n\geq 1}
is
C
-tight.

Proof.
It suffices to prove the two inequalities in (
4.103
) hold for
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,2}\}_{n\geq 1}
.
The first inequality holds obviously since
\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,2}(0)\overset{\rm a.s.}{=}0
for each
n\geq 1
.
For the second inequality, by the power inequality we have for
p>2
and any
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,2}(t+\delta)-\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,2}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J_{4,1}^{(n)}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]+C\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J_{4,2}^{(n)}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]

(4.118)

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
p
, where

\displaystyle J_{4,1}^{(n)}(t,\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{t+\delta-s-y/n}^{t+\delta-s}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)dr\Big)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy),

\displaystyle J_{4,2}^{(n)}(t,\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{t+\delta-s-y/n}^{t+\delta-s}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)dr-\int_{t-s-y/n}^{t-s}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)dr\Big)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{t-s-y/n}^{t-s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)dr\Big)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy).

By using (
2.7
), Lemma
4.19
and then the change of variables, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J_{4,1}^{(n)}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\bigg|n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\delta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)dr\Big)^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,ds\,dy\bigg|^{p}

\displaystyle+C\cdot n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\delta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)dr\Big)^{2p}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,ds\,dy.

By Lemma
4.4
, we have
\int_{s-y/n}^{s}R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)dr\leq\frac{1}{c}(s\wedge\frac{y}{n})
for any
s,y\geq 0
and then

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J_{4,1}^{(n)}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\bigg|n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\delta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(s\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,ds\,dy\bigg|^{p}

(4.120)

\displaystyle+C\cdot n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\delta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(s\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{2p}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,ds\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\bigg|n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\delta\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,ds\,dy\bigg|^{p}\cdot\delta^{p}

(4.122)

\displaystyle+C\cdot n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\delta\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\cdot\delta^{2p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\big(\delta^{p}+\delta^{2p-1}\big),

(4.124)

uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
.
Here the last inequality follows from (
4.42
).
Similarly,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J_{4,2}^{(n)}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\Big(\big|I^{(n)}_{2}(\delta)\big|^{p}+I^{(n)}_{2p}(\delta)\Big),

(4.125)

for some constant
C>0
independent of
n
, where

\displaystyle I^{(n)}_{2k}(\delta):=n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)dr\Big)^{2k}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy,\quad k\geq 1.

By Hölder’s inequality and Corollary
4.13
, we have uniformly in
s\in[0,T]
,
\delta\in(0,1)
and
y\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)dr\Big)^{2k}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big|dr\Big)^{2}\Big(\int_{-1}^{T}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big|dr\Big)^{2k-2}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big|dr\Big)^{2}\Big((T+1)\int_{-1}^{T}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big|^{2}dr\Big)^{k-1}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}\big(n(r+\delta)\big)-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big|dr\Big)^{2}\cdot\delta^{k-1}.

Plugging this back into
I^{(n)}_{2k}
, we have

\displaystyle I^{(n)}_{2k}(\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\delta^{k-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}n^{2}\theta_{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)dr\Big|^{2}ds

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\delta^{k-1}\cdot\big(A^{(n)}_{1}(\delta)+A^{(n)}_{2}(\delta)\big),

where

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{1}(\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{nT}^{\infty}\,\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)dr\Big|^{2}\,ds,

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{2}(\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{nT}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\int_{0}^{T}\Big|\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)dr\Big|^{2}\,ds.

By the fact that
R_{\varPi_{n}}(x)=0
for
x<0
and Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{1}(\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{nT}^{\infty}T\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\cdot\Big|\int_{-1}^{T}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)dr\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{nT}^{\infty}(T+1)^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\cdot\int_{-1}^{T}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big((T+1)\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\cdot\int_{-1}^{T}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big|^{2}dr,

which can be bounded by
C\cdot\delta
uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
because of Corollary
4.13
and (
4.42
).
For
A^{(n)}_{2}(\delta)
, using Hölder’s inequality again gives that

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{2}(\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\cdot\int_{0}^{nT}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)^{2}\,dr\Big)\cdot\frac{y}{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy,

which can be further divided into the following two parts

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{2,1}(\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\cdot\int_{0}^{nT}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{(s-y/n)\wedge 0}^{0}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big|^{2}\,dr\Big)\cdot\frac{y}{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy,

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{2,2}(\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\cdot\int_{0}^{nT}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{(s-y/n)\vee 0}^{s}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big|^{2}\,dr\Big)\cdot\frac{y}{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy.

The fact that
R_{\varPi_{n}}(x)=0
for
x<0
induces that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{(s-y/n)\wedge 0}^{0}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big|^{2}\,dr\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y/n}\int_{-(s\wedge\delta)}^{0}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))\big)^{2}drds

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y/n}\frac{s\wedge\delta}{c^{2}}ds\leq\frac{\delta}{c^{2}}\cdot\frac{y}{n},

which along with Proposition
4.14
yields that

\displaystyle A^{(n)}_{2,1}(\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\delta}{c^{2}}\cdot n^{2}\theta_{n}\cdot\int_{0}^{nT}\Big(\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{2}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)dy

(4.126)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{\delta}{c^{2}}\cdot n^{2}\theta_{n}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(T\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{2}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)dy\leq C\cdot\delta.

(4.127)

Moreover, an application of Fubini’s theorem to the inner integral of
A^{(n)}_{2,2}(\delta)
induces that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{(s-y/n)\vee 0}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(r+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)\big)^{2}\,dr\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{y}{n}\int_{0}^{T}\big|R_{\varPi_{n}}(n(s+\delta))-R_{\varPi_{n}}(ns)\big|^{2}\,ds.

Similarly as in (
4.126
), we also have
A^{(n)}_{2,2}(\delta)\leq C\cdot\delta
uniformly in
n\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle I^{(n)}_{2k}(\delta)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\delta^{k-1}\cdot\Big(A^{(n)}_{1}(\delta)+A^{(n)}_{2,1}(\delta)+A^{(n)}_{2,2}(\delta)\Big)\leq C\cdot\delta^{k}.

Plugging this result with
k=1
and
p
into (
4.125
) yields that uniformly in
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|J_{4,2}^{(n)}(t,\delta)\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\delta^{p},

Taking this and (
4.120
) back into (
4.118
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,2}(t+\delta)-\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,2}\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\delta^{p}

and then the sequence
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4,2}\}_{n\geq 1}
is
C
-tight.

\Box

Corollary 4.32

The sequence
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4}\}_{n\geq 1}
is
C
-tight.

4.4
Limit characterization

Armed with the
C
-tightness results proved in the last section, we now turn to characterize their limits, which will be used to derive the stochastic Volterra representation for the process
L^{\xi}_{\zeta}
.
By Lemma
4.22
and
4.25
, cluster points of
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{1}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{2}\}_{n\geq 1}
are clear and it remains to characterize the limits of
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
\{\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{4}\}_{n\geq 1}
.
Note that integrands in (
4.19
) and (
4.20
) vary in
n
, which makes their limit characterizations much challenging.
To overcome this difficulty, we provide some good approximations for them in the next section.

4.4.1
Good approximations for
\{\mathbf{I}_{3}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
\{\mathbf{I}_{4}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}

The second limit in Lemma
4.5
tells that
R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr,y)\sim y\cdot W^{\prime}(r)
as
n\to\infty
. Hence it is sensible to conjecture that the sequence
\{\mathbf{I}_{3,2}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
can be asymptotically approximated by
\{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
with

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}^{(n)}_{3}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{y}{n}\cdot W^{\prime}(t-s)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy),\quad t\geq 0.

For each
n\geq 1
, we consider the following two integrated processes

\displaystyle I_{\mathbf{I}_{3}}^{(n)}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{3}(s)\,ds\quad\mbox{and}\quad I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}^{(n)}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}^{(n)}_{3}(s)\,ds,\quad t\geq 0,

Lemma 4.33

The following hold.

(1)

The sequence
\{(I_{\mathbf{I}_{3}}^{(n)},I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}^{(n)})\}_{n\geq 1}

is
C
-tight.

(2)

For each
T\geq 0
, we have
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|I_{\mathbf{I}_{3}}^{(n)}(t)-I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}^{(n)}(t)\big|\overset{\rm p}{\to}0
as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.
The first claim follows directly from the second one and Corollary
4.29
. We now prove the second claim.
For convention, we denote by
\{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{3}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
the error process, i.e.,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{3}^{(n)}(t):=\mathbf{I}_{3}^{(n)}(t)-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}^{(n)}_{3}(t),\quad t\geq 0.

By the triangle inequality and the decomposition of
\mathbf{I}_{3}^{(n)}
; see (
4.77
)-(
4.78
),

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|I_{\mathbf{I}_{3}}^{(n)}(t)-I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}^{(n)}(t)\big|\leq\big\|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{3}^{(n)}\big\|_{L^{1}_{T}}\leq\big\|\mathbf{I}_{3,1}^{(n)}\big\|_{L^{1}_{T}}+\big\|\mathbf{I}_{3,2}^{(n)}-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}^{(n)}_{3}\big\|_{L^{1}_{T}}.

Firstly, Lemma
4.27
along with the continuous mapping theorem induces that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{I}_{3,1}^{(n)}(s)\,ds\to 0,

weakly in
C(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
as
n\to\infty
, which directly yields that

\big\|\mathbf{I}_{3,1}^{(n)}\big\|_{L^{1}_{T}}\to 0
in distribution and hence in probability.
Secondly, by using (
2.7
) along with Lemma
4.4
and then the change of variables, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
n
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}_{3,2}^{(n)}(t)-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}^{(n)}_{3}(t)\big|^{2}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(R_{n}\big(n(t-s),y\big)-y\cdot W^{\prime}(t-s)\Big)^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,ds\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\int_{0}^{T}\big(R_{n}\big(ns,y\big)-y\cdot W^{\prime}(s)\big)^{2}\,ds,

which goes to
0
by the dominated convergence theorem and (
4.40
). By Hölder’s inequality, Fubini’s theorem and the dominated convergence theorem, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big\|\mathbf{I}_{3,2}^{(n)}-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}^{(n)}_{3}\big\|_{L^{1}_{T}}^{2}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle T\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\big\|\mathbf{I}_{3,2}^{(n)}-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}^{(n)}_{3}\big\|_{L^{2}_{T}}^{2}\Big]=T\cdot\int_{0}^{T}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}_{3,2}^{(n)}(t)-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}^{(n)}_{3}(t)\big|^{2}\Big]dt\to 0,

as
n\to\infty
and hence
\big\|\mathbf{I}_{3,2}^{(n)}-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}^{(n)}_{3}\big\|_{L^{1}_{T}}\to 0
in distribution and hence in probability.

\Box

By the first limit in Lemma
4.5
, we also see that
R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)
can be well-approximated by
W^{\prime}(r)
as
n\to\infty
, which allows us to approximate the sequence
\{\mathbf{I}_{4,2}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
by
\{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
with

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}^{(n)}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{t-s-y/n}^{t-s}W^{\prime}(r)dr\Big)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy),\quad t\geq 0.

(4.128)

Lemma 4.34

The following hold.

(1)

The sequence
\{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
is
C
-tight.

(2)

For each
T\geq 0
, we have
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\mathbf{I}_{4}^{(n)}(t)-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}^{(n)}_{4}(t)\big|\overset{\rm p}{\to}0
as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.
The first claim can be proved by repeating the proof of Lemma
4.31
with
R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)
replaced by
W^{\prime}(r)
.
For the second claim, by the decomposition of
\mathbf{I}_{4}^{(n)}
(see (
4.116
)-(
4.117
)) and Lemma
4.30
, it remains to prove that as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\mathbf{I}_{4,2}^{(n)}(t)-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}^{(n)}(t)\big|\overset{\rm p}{\to}0.

By the first claim and Lemma
4.31
, it suffices to prove that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}_{4,2}^{(n)}(t)-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}^{(n)}(t)\big|^{2}\Big]=0.

Indeed, by (
4.116
) and (
4.117
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{4,2}^{(n)}(t)-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}^{(n)}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{t-s-y/n}^{t-s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)-W^{\prime}(r)\big)dr\Big)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy).

By using (
2.7
), Lemma
4.19
and the change of variables,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}_{4,2}^{(n)}(t)-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}^{(n)}(t)\big|^{2}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{s-y/n}^{s}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)-W^{\prime}(r)\big)dr\Big)^{2}\,ds.

Similar to establishing the estimates of
I^{(n)}_{2k}
in the proof of Lemma
4.31
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}_{4,2}^{(n)}(t)-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}^{(n)}(t)\big|^{2}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle n^{2}\theta_{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(t\wedge\frac{y}{n}\Big)^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,dy\cdot\int_{0}^{T}\big(R_{\varPi_{n}}(nr)-W^{\prime}(r)\big)^{2}dr,

which vanishes as
n\to\infty
; see (
4.40
) and (
4.42
).

\Box

4.4.2
Convergence of stochastic Volterra integrals

By Lemma
4.33
and
4.34
, we can characterize the limits of
\{I_{\mathbf{I}_{3}}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
\{\mathbf{I}_{4}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
by
identifying the weak limit of the two sequences
\{I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
\{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
.
Applying the stochastic Fubini’s theorem along with (
1.7
) to
I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}^{(n)}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}^{(n)}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}dr\int_{0}^{r}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{y}{n}\cdot W^{\prime}(r-s)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy)

(4.129)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{y}{n}\cdot W(t-s)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{c}(ds,dz,dy),\quad t\geq 0,

(4.130)

which together with (
4.128
) motivates us to establish the weak convergence theorem for the following stochastic Volterra integral

\displaystyle Z^{(n)}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}G\big(t-s,y/n\big)\widetilde{N}^{(n)}(ds,dz,dy),\quad t\geq 0.

for some process
X^{(n)}\in D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})
, function
G
on
(0,\infty)^{2}
and compensated Poisson random measure
\widetilde{N}^{(n)}(ds,dz,dy)
on
(0,\infty)^{3}
with intensity
ds\,dz\,\mu_{n}(dy)
and
\mu_{n}(dy)
being a
\sigma
-finite measure on
(0,\infty)
.

Lemma 4.35

Assume that
G(t,y)=F(t)\cdot y
for some locally bounded function
F
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
If

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{y^{2}}{n^{2}}\,\mu_{n}(dy)\to\sigma^{2}\in(0,\infty)\quad\mbox{and}\quad X^{(n)}\to X^{*}\in C(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})\mbox{ weakly in $D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})$},

(4.131)

as
n\to\infty
, we have
Z^{(n)}\overset{\rm f.d.d.}{\longrightarrow}Z^{*}
with the limit process
Z^{*}
given by

\displaystyle Z^{*}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X^{*}(s)}F(t-s)B^{*}(ds,dz),\quad t\geq 0,

where
B^{*}(ds,dz)
is Gaussian white noise with intensity
\sigma^{2}\cdot ds\,dz
.

Proof.
It suffices to prove that for any
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
0\leq t_{1}<\cdots<t_{d}
,

\displaystyle\big(Z^{(n)}(t_{1}),\cdots,Z^{(n)}(t_{d})\big)\overset{\rm d}{\to}\big(Z^{*}(t_{1}),\cdots,Z^{*}(t_{d})\big),

(4.132)

as
n\to\infty
.
However, the dependence of the integrand
G
on the time variable does not allow us prove it by using the convergence theorem for Itô’s integrals directly.
To “drop” the dependence of integrand on the time variable, we consider instead the integral processes

\displaystyle Z_{i}^{(n)}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}F(t_{i}-s)\cdot\frac{y}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}(ds,dz,dy),

(4.133)

\displaystyle Z^{*}_{i}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X(s)}F(t_{i}-s)B^{*}(ds,dz),\quad t\geq 0,\,i=1,\cdots,d.

(4.134)

It is obvious that
Z^{(n)}(t_{i})\overset{\rm a.s.}{=}Z_{i}^{(n)}(t_{i})
and
Z^{*}(t_{i})\overset{\rm a.s.}{=}Z^{*}_{i}(t_{i})
for all
i=1,\cdots,d
.
By Proposition 3.14 in
[
40
, p.349]
and the continuity of
\big(Z^{*}_{1},...,Z^{*}_{d}\big)
, we can obtain (
4.132
) by proving that

\displaystyle\big(Z^{(n)}_{1},...,Z^{(n)}_{d}\big)\to\big(Z^{*}_{1},...,Z^{*}_{d}\big),

(4.135)

weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
n\to\infty
.

We now prove (
4.135
) by using Lemma
A.4
with
\mathbb{H}=L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
.
We first write (
4.133
) and (
4.134
) into the form of stochastic integrals driven by
(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}))^{\#}
-martingales.
For
i=1,\cdots,d
, we define a mapping
\mathbf{F}_{i}
from
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})\times[0,\infty)
into
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
by

\displaystyle(x,s)\mapsto\mathbf{F}_{i}(x,s)(z):=F(t_{i}-s)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{0<z\leq x(s)\}}.

(4.136)

and introduce two standard
(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}))^{\#}
-martingales

\displaystyle{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(t):=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{y}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}\big((0,t],dz,dy\big)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{B}^{*}(t):=B^{*}\big((0,t],dz\big),\quad t\geq 0.

(4.137)

On terms of these notation, we can represent the two integral processes
Z^{(n)}_{i}
and
Z^{*}_{i}
as

\displaystyle Z^{(n)}_{i}(t)=\mathbf{F}_{i}(X^{(n)},-)\cdot d{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(t)\quad\mbox{and}\quad Z^{*}_{i}(t)=\mathbf{F}_{i}(X^{*},-)\cdot d\mathbf{B}^{*}(t),\quad t\geq 0.

By Lemma
A.4
, the weak convergence (
4.135
) holds if we can prove the following two claims.

\bullet

The sequence
\{{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
is uniformly tight;

\bullet

(\mathbf{F}_{1}(X^{(n)},\cdot),\cdots,\mathbf{F}_{d}(X^{(n)},\cdot),{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)})\Rightarrow(\mathbf{F}_{1}(X^{*},\cdot),\cdots,\mathbf{F}_{d}(X^{*},\cdot),\mathbf{B}^{*})
.

Uniform tightness.
Recall Definition
A.3
(1). It suffices to prove that for any
T\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any càdlàg
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
-valued process
U
with
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|U(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1
a.s.,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|U(-)\cdot d{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(t)\big|^{2}\Big]\leq C.

By (
4.137
) and then the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality,

\displaystyle U(-)\cdot d{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}U(s-,z)\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{y}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}\big(ds,dz,dy\big)

and there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
n,T,U
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|U(-)\cdot d{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(t)\big|^{2}\bigg]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\mathbf{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|U(s-,z)\big|^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{y^{2}}{n^{2}}\,N^{(n)}\big(ds,dz,dy\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\mathbf{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|U(s-,z)\big|^{2}dz\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{y^{2}}{n^{2}}\,\mu_{n}(dy)\bigg],

which is bounded uniformly in
n
and
U
because of the assumption of
U
and the first limit in (
4.131
).
Hence the first claim holds.

Weak convergence.
By Definition
A.3
(2), it suffices to prove that for any
k\geq 1
and
f_{1},\cdots,f_{k}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{F}_{1}(X^{(n)},\cdot),\cdots,\mathbf{F}_{d}(X^{(n)},\cdot),{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(f_{1}),\cdots,{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(f_{k})\big)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\to\!\!\!

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{F}_{1}(X^{*},\cdot),\cdots,\mathbf{F}_{d}(X^{*},\cdot),\mathbf{B}^{*}(f_{1}),\cdots,\mathbf{B}^{*}(f_{k})\big),

weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}))^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{k})
as
n\to\infty
.
For simplicity, we just prove it with
d=k=1
.
The general case can be proved in the same way.
Note that
\mathbf{B}^{*}(f_{1})\in C(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
and the continuity of
X^{*}
is inherited by
\mathbf{F}_{1}(\xi^{*},\cdot)
.
Moreover, the function
f_{1}
can be write as
f_{1}^{+}-f_{1}^{-}
with
f_{1}^{+},f_{1}^{-}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})
.
By Corollary 3.33 in
[
40
, p.353]
, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{1}\big(X^{(n)},\cdot\big)\to\mathbf{F}_{1}\big(X^{*},\cdot\big)\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(f_{1})\to\mathbf{B}^{*}(f_{1}),

(4.138)

weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}))
and
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
separately for
f_{1}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})
.
By the Skorokhod representation theorem, we may assume
X^{(n)}\overset{\rm a.s.}{\to}X^{*}
in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})
.
The continuity of
X^{*}
induces that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|X^{(n)}(t)-X^{*}(t)\big|\overset{\rm a.s.}{\to}0

as
n\to 0
for any
T\geq 0
; see Proposition 1.17(b) in
[
40
, p.328]
. This along with (
4.136
) induces that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\|\mathbf{F}_{1}(X^{(n)},t)-\mathbf{F}_{1}(X^{*},t)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|F(t_{i}-t)|^{2}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\mathbf{1}_{\{0<z\leq X^{(n)}(t)\}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{0<z\leq X^{*}(t)\}}\big|^{2}\,dz

(4.139)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|F(t_{i}-t)|^{2}\cdot\big|X^{(n)}(t)-X^{*}(t)\big|

(4.140)

which goes to
0
almost surely as
n\to\infty
and hence the first limit in (
4.138
) holds.
For the second one, note that

\displaystyle{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(f_{1},t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}f_{1}(z)\cdot\frac{y}{n}\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}\big(ds,dz,dy\big),\quad t\geq 0,

is a spectrally positive Lévy process with Laplace exponent

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(\exp\Big\{-\lambda\cdot f_{1}(z)\cdot\frac{y}{n}\Big\}-1+\lambda\cdot f_{1}(z)\cdot\frac{y}{n}\bigg)\,dz\,\mu_{n}(dy),\quad\lambda\geq 0,

which, by the first limit in (
4.131
), converges as
n\to\infty
to

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|f_{1}(z)\big|^{2}\,dz\cdot\lambda^{2}.

This together with Corollary 4.3 in
[
40
, p.440]
and Theorem III-7 in
[
28
]
induces that

\displaystyle{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(f_{1})\to\sigma\cdot\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|f_{1}(z)\big|^{2}\,dz\Big)^{1/2}\cdot B\overset{\rm d}{=}\mathbf{B}^{*}(f_{1}),

weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
as
n\to\infty
, where
B
is a standard Brownian motion.
The second claim holds.

\Box

Lemma 4.36

Assume that there exists a
\sigma
-finite measure
m(dy)
on
(0,\infty)
such that for any
T>0
and non-negative measurable function
f
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|G(t,y)\big|^{2}\,m(dy)<\infty\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sup_{n\geq 1}\int_{0}^{\infty}f(y)\,\mu_{n}(n\cdot dy)\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}f(y)\,m(dy).

(4.141)

If
X^{(n)}\to X^{*}\in C(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})
weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})
and
\mu_{n}(n\cdot dy)\to\mu^{*}(dy)
vaguely as
n\to\infty
, we have
Z^{(n)}\overset{\rm f.d.d.}{\longrightarrow}Z^{*}
with the limit process
Z^{*}
given by

\displaystyle Z^{*}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X^{*}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}G(t-s,y)\,N^{*}(ds,dz,dy),\quad t\geq 0,

where
N^{*}(ds,dz,dy)
is a Poisson random measure on
(0,\infty)^{3}
with intensity
ds\,dz\,\mu^{*}(dy)
.

Proof.
Similarly as in the proof of Lemma
4.35
, for any
d\geq 1
and
0\leq t_{1}<\cdots<t_{d}
it suffices to prove that

\big(Z^{(n)}_{1},...,Z^{(n)}_{d}\big)\to\big(Z^{*}_{1},...,Z^{*}_{d}\big)

weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}^{k})
as
n\to\infty
, where

\displaystyle Z_{i}^{(n)}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X^{(n)}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}G(t_{i}-s,y)\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}(ds,dz,n\cdot dy),

(4.142)

\displaystyle Z^{*}_{i}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}G(t_{i}-s,y)N^{*}(ds,dz,dy),\quad t\geq 0,\,i=1,\cdots,d.

(4.143)

Let
\mathbf{m}(dz,dy):=dz\,m(dy)
and
L^{2}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2};\mathbb{R})
be the Hilbert space of all square integrable functions on
(0,\infty)^{2}
with respect to
\mathbf{m}(dz,dy)
endowed with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbf{m}}}
.
For
i=1,\cdots,d
, we define a mapping
\mathbf{G}_{i}
from
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})\times[0,\infty)
into
L^{2}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2};\mathbb{R})
by

\displaystyle(x,s)\mapsto\mathbf{G}_{i}(x,s)(z,y):=G(t_{i}-s,y)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{0<z\leq x(s)\}}.

and introduce two standard
(L^{2}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2};\mathbb{R}))^{\#}
-martingales

\displaystyle{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(t):=\widetilde{N}^{(n)}\big((0,t],dz,n\cdot dy\big)\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{*}(t):=\widetilde{N}^{*}\big((0,t],dz,dy\big),\quad t\geq 0.

(4.144)

Similarly, we can write the two equations (
4.142
) and (
4.143
) as

\displaystyle Z^{(n)}_{i}(t)=\mathbf{G}_{i}(X^{(n)},-)\cdot d{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(t)\quad\mbox{and}\quad Z^{*}_{i}(t)=\mathbf{G}_{i}(X^{*},-)\cdot d{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{*}(t),\quad t\geq 0.

By Lemma
A.4
with
\mathbb{H}=L^{2}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2};\mathbb{R})
, it suffices to prove the uniform tightness of
\{{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}\}_{n\geq 1}
and

\displaystyle(\mathbf{G}_{1}(X^{(n)},\cdot),\cdots,\mathbf{G}_{d}(X^{(n)},\cdot),{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)})\Rightarrow(\mathbf{G}_{1}(X^{*},\cdot),\cdots,\mathbf{G}_{d}(X^{*},\cdot),{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{*}).

Uniform tightness.
Recall Definition
A.3
(1). It suffices to prove that for any
T\geq 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any càdlàg
L^{2}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2};\mathbb{R})
-valued process
U
with
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|U(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbf{m}}}\leq 1
a.s.,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\mathbf{E}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|U(-)\cdot d{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(t)\big|^{2}\Big]\leq C.

By (
4.144
) and then the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality,

\displaystyle U(-)\cdot d{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}U(s-,z,y)\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}\big(ds,dz,n\cdot dy\big)

and there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
n,T,U
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|U(-)\cdot d{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(t)\big|^{2}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|U(s-,z,y)\big|^{2}\,N^{(n)}\big(ds,dz,n\cdot dy\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}dz\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|U(s-,z,y)\big|^{2}\,\mu_{n}(n\cdot dy)\Big].

which, by the first inequality in (
4.141
) and the fact that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|U(t)\|_{L^{2}_{\mathbf{m}}}\leq 1
a.s, can be bounded uniformly in
n
and
U
by

\displaystyle C\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}dz\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|U(s-,z,y)\big|^{2}\,m(dy)\Big]\leq C\cdot T.

Weak convergence.

Similarly as in the end of the proof of Lemma
4.35
, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{1}(\xi^{(n)},\cdot)\to\mathbf{G}_{1}(\xi^{*},\cdot)\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(f_{1})\to{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{*}(f_{1})

weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2};\mathbb{R}))
and
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
separately for
f_{1}\in L^{2}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2};\mathbb{R}_{+})
.
The first limit can be proved similarly as in (
4.139
).
For the second one, note that

\displaystyle{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(f_{1},t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}f_{1}(z,y)\,\widetilde{N}^{(n)}\big(ds,dz,n\cdot dy\big),\quad t\geq 0,

is a spectrally positive Lévy process with Laplace exponent

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(e^{-\lambda\cdot f_{1}(z,y)}-1+\lambda\cdot f_{1}(z,y)\big)\,dz\,\mu_{n}(n\cdot dy),\quad\lambda\geq 0,

which, by the assumption that
\mu_{n}(n\cdot dy)\to\mu^{*}(dy)
vaguely as
n\to\infty
, converges as
n\to\infty
to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(e^{-\lambda\cdot f_{1}(z,y)}-1+\lambda\cdot f_{1}(z,y)\big)\,dz\,\mu^{*}(dy).

This together with Theorem III-7 in
[
28
]
induces that

{\bf\widetilde{N}}^{(n)}(f_{1})\to\mathbf{N}^{*}(f_{1})
.

\Box

4.4.3
Proof of Theorem
1.1

The proof is carried out in the next three steps.
The uniqueness of solution to (
1.9
) will be proved in the end of Section
8.2
.

Step 1:
C
-tightness.
An application of Corollary 3.33 in
[
40
, p.353]
along with Lemma
4.22
,

4.25
,
4.33
,
4.34
and Corollary
4.29
,

4.32
immediately induces the
C
-tightness of the sequence

\displaystyle\big\{\big(X_{\zeta}^{(n)},\mathbf{I}_{1}^{(n)},\mathbf{I}_{2}^{(n)},\mathbf{I}_{3}^{(n)},I_{\mathbf{I}_{3}}^{(n)},I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}^{(n)},\mathbf{I}_{4}^{(n)},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}^{(n)}_{4}\big)\big\}_{n\geq 1}.

(4.145)

Step 2: Limit characterization.
Assume that
\big(X_{\zeta},\mathbf{I}_{1},\mathbf{I}_{2},\mathbf{I}_{3},I_{\mathbf{I}_{3}},I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}},\mathbf{I}_{4},\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}\big)
is a cluster point of the sequence (
4.145
).
We now characterize the limit processes one-by-one.

\bullet

By Lemma
4.22
and
4.25
, we first have

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{1}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\zeta\cdot c\cdot W(t)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{I}_{2}(t)=\int_{0}^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(t)-W(t-y)\big)N_{0}(dz,dy),\quad t\geq 0.\qquad

(4.146)

\bullet

An application of Lemma
4.35
along with the two facts that
X_{\zeta}^{(n)}\to X_{\zeta}
weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+})
and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{y^{2}}{n^{2}}\cdot n^{2}\gamma_{n}(1-\theta_{n})\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,\cdot ds\,dz\,dy\sim\frac{1}{c}\int_{0}^{\infty}y^{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,dy\to 2c

to the stochastic integral (
4.129
) induces that the limit process
I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}
admits the representation

\displaystyle I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}W(t-s)B_{c}(ds,dz),\quad t\geq 0.

Using the stochastic Fubini’s theorem (
2.8
) along with (
1.7
) to the preceding stochastic integral, we have

\displaystyle I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{t-s}W(r)drB_{c}(ds,dz)=\int_{0}^{t}dr\int_{0}^{r}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}W^{\prime}(r-s)B_{c}(ds,dz).

Moreover, applying the continuous mapping theorem to the weak convergence
(I_{\mathbf{I}_{3}}^{(n)},I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}^{(n)})\to(I_{\mathbf{I}_{3}},I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}})
and then using Lemma
4.33
(2),

\displaystyle I_{\mathbf{I}_{3}}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{I}_{3}(s)ds=I_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}(t),\quad t\geq 0,

which along with the continuity of
\mathbf{I}_{3}
induces that

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{3}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}W^{\prime}(t-s)B_{c}(ds,dz),\quad t\geq 0.

(4.147)

\bullet

By Lemma
4.34
, we have
\mathbf{I}_{4}=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}
.
By the proof of Proposition
4.2
,

\displaystyle n^{3}\gamma_{n}\theta_{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(ny)\,\cdot ds\,dz\,dy\to ds\,dz\,\nu(dy),

vaguely
n\to\infty
.
By Lemma
4.36
and the weak convergence
X_{\zeta}^{(n)}\to X_{\zeta}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{4}(t)=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{I}}_{4}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(t-s)-W(t-s-y)\big)\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy),\quad t\geq 0.

(4.148)

Step 3: Stochastic Volterra equation.

Notice that the limit process
(X_{\zeta},\mathbf{I}_{1},\mathbf{I}_{2},\mathbf{I}_{3},\mathbf{I}_{4})
is determined uniquely in distribution by
X_{\zeta}
, which, by Corollary
3.5
, always equals in distribution to
L^{\xi}_{\zeta}
.
In conclusion, we have as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\big(X_{\zeta}^{(n)},\mathbf{I}_{1}^{(n)},\mathbf{I}_{2}^{(n)},\mathbf{I}_{3}^{(n)},\mathbf{I}_{4}^{(n)}\big)\to\big(X_{\zeta},\mathbf{I}_{1},\mathbf{I}_{2},\mathbf{I}_{3},\mathbf{I}_{4}\big)\in C(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}),

weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
By Proposition 2.4 in
[
40
, p.339]
and the continuous mapping theorem,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigg|X_{\zeta}(t)-\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathbf{I}_{i}(t)\bigg|\!\!\!

\displaystyle\overset{\rm d}{=}\!\!\!

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigg|X_{\zeta}^{(n)}(t)-\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathbf{I}_{i}^{(n)}(t)\bigg|\overset{\rm a.s.}{=}0,

for any
T\geq 0
, which yields that

\displaystyle X_{\zeta}(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathbf{I}_{i}(t),\quad t\geq 0.

(4.149)

Plugging (
4.146
)-(
4.148
) into the right-hand side, we can get (
1.9
) immediately.

4.4.4
Proof of Theorem
1.5

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\zeta_{1}\leq\zeta_{2}
.
Consider two sequences of compound Poission processes
\{Y_{1,n}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
\{Y_{2,n}\}_{n\geq 1}
, in which
Y_{i,n}
,
i=1,2
, has a drift
-1
, jump-size distribution
\varPi_{n}(dy)
with density
\pi_{n}
given by
(
4.5
)
and arrival rate
\gamma_{i,n}
defined by (
4.4
) with
b=b_{i}
.
For
t\geq 0
, let
\xi^{(n)}_{i}(t):=Y_{i,n}(n^{2}t)/n
that is a spectrally positive Lévy process with Laplace exponent denoted by
\varPhi^{(n)}_{i}
given by (
4.11
) with
\gamma_{n}=\gamma_{i,n}
.
Repeating the proof of Proposition
4.2
shows that
\varPhi^{(n)}_{i}\to\varPhi_{i}
pointwisely with
\varPhi_{i}
being the Laplace exponent of
\xi_{i}
and then
\xi^{(n)}_{i}\to\xi_{i}
weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
as
n\to\infty
.

For each
n\geq 1
, let
N^{n}_{c}(ds,dz,dy,du)
and
N^{n}_{\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy,du)
be two orthogonal
(\mathscr{F}_{nt})
-compensated Poisson random measures on
(0,\infty)^{4}
with intensities

\displaystyle(1-\theta_{n})\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,\cdot ds\,dz\,dy\,du\quad\mbox{and}\quad\theta_{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,\cdot ds\,dz\,dy\,du.

Let
B_{n}^{c}(\zeta_{1})
and
B_{n}^{c}(\zeta_{2}-\zeta_{1})
be two independent binomial random variables with common success probability
p_{n}
and number of trials
[n\zeta_{1}]
and
[n\zeta_{2}]-[n\zeta_{1}]
respectively.
Moreover, let
B^{c}_{n}(\zeta_{2}):=B^{c}_{n}(\zeta_{1})+B_{n}^{c}(\zeta_{2}-\zeta_{1})
,
B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta_{1}):=[n\zeta_{1}]-B^{c}_{n}(\zeta_{1})
and
B_{n}^{\Lambda}(\zeta_{2}):=[n\zeta_{2}]-B^{c}_{n}(\zeta_{2})
.
For
i=1,2
, let

N_{\varPi_{i}}^{n}(ds,dz,dy):=N_{c}^{n}(ds,dz,dy,[0,\gamma_{i,n}])+N_{\Lambda}^{n}(ds,dz,dy,[0,\gamma_{i,n}]).

By Lemma
3.1
, the process
L^{Y_{n,i}}_{[n\zeta_{i}]}
equals in distribution to the unique strong solution
Z^{i,n}_{[n\zeta_{i}]}
to

\displaystyle Z^{i,n}_{[n\zeta_{i}]}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{B^{c}_{n}(\zeta_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell^{c}_{n,j}>t\}}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{Z^{i,n}_{[n\zeta_{i}]}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{y>t-s\}}\,N_{c}^{n}(ds,dz,dy,[0,\gamma_{i,n}])

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{B^{\Lambda}_{n}(\zeta_{i})}\mathbf{1}_{\{\ell^{\Lambda}_{n,j}>t\}}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{Z^{i,n}_{[n\zeta_{i}]}(s-)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{y>t-s\}}\,N_{\Lambda}^{n}(ds,dz,dy,[0,\gamma_{i,n}]),\quad t\geq 0.

Similarly as in (
3.9
), we also have

L^{\xi^{(n)}_{i}}_{\zeta_{i}}\overset{\rm d}{=}X^{b_{i},(n)}_{\zeta_{i}}=\big\{n^{-1}\cdot Z^{i,n}_{[n\zeta_{i}]}(nt):t\geq 0\big\}.

The proof of the next lemma is elementary and omitted.

Lemma 4.37

If
b_{1}\geq b_{2}
, we have for any
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(X^{b_{1},(n)}_{\zeta_{1}}(t)\leq X^{b_{2},(n)}_{\zeta_{2}}(t),\,t\geq 0\Big)=\mathbf{P}\Big(Z^{1,n}_{[n\zeta_{1}]}(t)\leq Z^{2,n}_{[n\zeta_{2}]}(t),\,t\geq 0\Big)=1.

Similarly as in (
4.17
)-(
4.20
), the process
(X^{b_{1},(n)}_{\zeta_{1}},X^{b_{2},(n)}_{\zeta_{2}})
is the unique solution to the following two dimensional stochastic Volterra equation

\displaystyle X_{\zeta}^{b_{i}(n)}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{4}\mathbf{I}^{(n)}_{i,j}(t),\quad t\geq 0,\,i=1,2,

with the summands are given as in (
4.18
)-(
4.20
), where the resolvent
R_{\varPi_{n},i}
and two parameter function
R_{i,n}
are defined as in (
4.21
) and (
4.22
) respectively with
\gamma_{n}=\gamma_{i,n}
, and

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{i,c}(ds,dz,dy,[0,\gamma_{i,n}])\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle N_{c}^{n}(n\cdot ds,n\cdot dz,dy,[0,\gamma_{i,n}])-n^{2}\gamma_{i,n}(1-\theta_{n})\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}(y)\,\cdot ds\,dz\,dy,

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}^{(n)}_{i,\Lambda}(ds,dz,dy,[0,\gamma_{i,n}])\!\!\!

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!

\displaystyle N^{n}_{\Lambda}(n\cdot ds,n\cdot dz,dy,[0,\gamma_{i,n}])-n^{2}\gamma_{i,n}\theta_{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{c}*d\Lambda_{n}(y)\,\cdot ds\,dz\,dy.

Repeating all arguments in Section
4.2
-
4.3
and the proof of Theorem
1.1
proves that

\Big(X^{b_{1},(n)}_{\zeta_{1}},X^{b_{2},(n)}_{\zeta_{2}}\Big)\to\Big(X^{b_{1}}_{\zeta_{1}},X^{b_{2}}_{\zeta_{2}}\Big),

weakly in
D(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})
as
n\to\infty
with
X_{\zeta_{i}}^{b_{i}}\overset{\rm d}{=}L^{\xi_{i}}_{\zeta_{i}}
for
i=1,2
, and there are three common driving noises
(N_{0},B_{c},\widetilde{N}_{\nu})
defined as in Theorem
1.1
such that the quadruple
(X^{b_{i}}_{\zeta_{i}},N_{0},B_{c},\widetilde{N}_{\nu})
satisfies (
1.9
) with characteristics
(\zeta_{i}\,;\,b_{i},c,\nu)
.
By Skorokhod’s representation theorem and then Proposition 1.17(b) in
[
40
, p.328]
, we may assume that

\big(X^{b_{1},(n)}_{\zeta_{1}},X^{b_{2},(n)}_{\zeta_{2}}\big)\overset{\rm a.s.}{\to}\big(X^{b_{1}}_{\zeta_{1}},X^{b_{2}}_{\zeta_{2}}\big),

uniformly on compacts as
n\to\infty
, which
along with Lemma
4.37
yields that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(X^{b_{1}}_{\zeta_{1}}(t)\leq X^{b_{2}}_{\zeta_{2}}(t),\,t\geq 0\Big)=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\Big(X^{b_{1},(n)}_{\zeta_{1}}(t)\leq X^{b_{2},(n)}_{\zeta_{2}}(t),\,t\geq 0\Big)=1

and the whole proof of Theorem
1.5
ends.

5
Strong existence and uniqueness

In this section, we prove the existence and uniqueness of strong solution to (
1.9
) under the assumption that
\bar{\bar{\nu}}(0)<\infty
.
Our proof replies on the next lemma, which is a direct consequence of Theorem 1.5 in
[
44
]
that generalizes the Yamada-Watanabe theorems established in
[
69
]
. The detailed proof is omitted.
We say
pathwise uniqueness
holds for (
1.9
) if any two solutions defined on the same filtrated probability space endowed with the same driving noises are distinguishable.

Lemma 5.1

If the existence of solutions and pathwise uniqueness hold for (
1.9
), then the strong solution exists uniquely.

Proof of Theorem
1.7
.

The existence of solutions to (
1.9
) follows from Theorem
1.1
.
Meanwhile, the next two lemmas respectively show that (
1.9
) is equivalent to (
1.24
) and pathwise uniqueness holds for (
1.24
).
In conclusion, there exists a unique strong solution to (
1.9
).

\Box

Lemma 5.2

The stochastic equation (
1.9
) is equivalent to (
1.24
).

Proof.
By the change of variables and the fact that
W^{\prime}(x)=0
for
x<0
,

\displaystyle W(t-s)-W(t-s-y)=\int_{t-s-y}^{t-s}W^{\prime}(r)dr=\int_{0}^{t}W^{\prime}(t-r)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{s<r\leq s+y\}}\,dr.

Plugging this into the last stochastic integral in (
1.9
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(t-s)-W(t-s-y)\big)\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}W^{\prime}(t-r)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{s<r\leq s+y\}}\,dr\Big)\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy).

The stochastic Fubini theorem (
2.9
) along with

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}W^{\prime}(t-r)\int_{0}^{r}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{s<r\leq s+y\}}\,\nu(dy)\,dr<\infty

induces that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(t-s)-W(t-s-y)\big)\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}W^{\prime}(t-r)\int_{0}^{r}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\{s<r\leq s+y\}}\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy)\,dr

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}W^{\prime}(x-r)\int_{0}^{r}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{r-s}^{\infty}\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy)\,dr.

Taking this back into (
1.9
) and then using the second equality in (
1.24
), it can be written as

\displaystyle X_{\zeta}(x)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\zeta\cdot c\cdot W^{\prime}(t)+\int_{0}^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(t-s)-W(t-s-y)\big)N_{0}(dz,dy)+\int_{0}^{t}W^{\prime}(t-s)dM(s).\quad

(5.1)

Since
W^{\prime}
is differentiable on
(0,\infty)
with
W^{\prime}(0)=1/c
, an application of the Fubini’s theorem to the last stochastic integral shows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}W^{\prime}(t-s)dM(s)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c}\cdot M(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t-s}W^{\prime\prime}(r)\,dr\,dM(s)

(5.2)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c}\cdot M(t)+\int_{0}^{t}W^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(t-s)M(s)\,ds.

(5.3)

Further, the differentiability of
W^{\prime\prime}
on
(0,\infty)
and
W^{\prime\prime}(0+)<\infty
induces that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}W^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(t-s)M(s)\,ds\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle W^{\prime\prime}(0+)\cdot M(t)+\int_{0}^{t}M(s)\int_{0}^{t-s}W^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle W^{\prime\prime}(0+)\cdot M(t)+\int_{0}^{t}W^{\prime\prime\prime}*M(s)\,ds.

The representation (
1.24
) follows immediately by taking this back into (
5.2
) and then (
5.1
).

\Box

Lemma 5.3

The pathwise uniqueness holds for (
1.24
).

Proof.
Assume that
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
are two solutions to (
1.9
) with common driving noises
(N_{0},B_{c},\widetilde{N}_{\nu})
.
Let
Z(t):=X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)
for
t\geq 0
.
By (
1.24
), we have

\displaystyle Z(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\Big(W^{\prime\prime}(0+)\cdot\overline{M}(s)+W^{\prime\prime\prime}*\overline{M}(s)\Big)\,ds+\frac{1}{c}\cdot\overline{M}(t),

(5.4)

is a
(\mathscr{F}_{t})
-semimartiangle, where

\displaystyle\overline{M}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{r-s}^{\infty}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{1}(s)\}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{2}(s)\}}\big)\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy)\,dr

(5.6)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{1}(s)\}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{2}(s)\}}\big)B_{c}(ds,dz).

Consider a strictly decreasing positive sequence
\{a_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
such that
\int_{a_{n}}^{a_{n-1}}z^{-1}dz=n
and
a_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, e.g.,

a_{n}=\exp\big\{-n(n+1)/2\big\}
.
Let
x\mapsto g_{n}(x)
be a non-negative continuous function on
\mathbb{R}
which has support in
(a_{n},a_{n-1})
and satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{a_{n}}^{a_{n-1}}g_{n}(x)\,dx=1\quad\mbox{and}\quad x\cdot g_{n}(x)\leq\frac{2}{n}.

Moreover, we define the non-negative and twice continuously differentiable function

\displaystyle f_{n}(z)=\int_{0}^{|z|}dy\int_{0}^{y}g_{n}(x)dx,\qquad z\in\mathbb{R}.

It is easy to see that
f_{n}(z)\rightarrow|z|
non-decreasingly as
n\to\infty
and

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}|f_{n}^{\prime}(z)|\leq 1\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}|zf^{\prime\prime}_{n}(z)|\leq\frac{2}{n},\quad n\geq 1.

(5.7)

By (
5.4
)-(
5.6
) and using Itô’s formula to
f_{n}\big(Z(t)\big)
; see Theorem 5.1 in
[
39
, p.66]
,

\displaystyle f_{n}\big(Z(t)\big)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}f^{\prime}_{n}\big(Z(s)\big)\Big(W^{\prime\prime}(0+)\cdot\overline{M}(s)+W^{\prime\prime\prime}*\overline{M}(s)\Big)\,ds+\frac{1}{c}\int_{0}^{t}\big|Z(s)\big|\cdot f^{\prime\prime}_{n}\big(Z(s)\big)\,ds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{c}\int_{0}^{t}f^{\prime}_{n}\big(Z(s)\big)\int_{0}^{s}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{s-r}^{\infty}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{1}(r)\}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{2}(r)\}}\big)\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(dr,dz,dy)\,ds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{c}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}f^{\prime}_{n}\big(Z(s)\big)\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{1}(s)\}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{2}(s)\}}\big)B_{c}(ds,dz).

By the non-negativity of
f_{n}
, (
5.7
) and the definition of compensated Poisson random measure,

\displaystyle f_{n}\big(Z(t)\big)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big|W^{\prime\prime}(0+)\cdot\overline{M}(s)+W^{\prime\prime\prime}*\overline{M}(s)\Big|\,ds+\frac{t}{c}\cdot\frac{2}{n}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{c}\int_{0}^{t}\bigg|\int_{0}^{s}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{s-r}^{\infty}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{1}(r)\}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{2}(r)\}}\big)\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(dr,dz,dy)\bigg|\,ds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{c}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}f^{\prime}_{n}\big(Z(s)\big)\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{1}(s)\}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{2}(s)\}}\big)B_{c}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\big|W^{\prime\prime}(0+)\big|\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\big|\overline{M}(s)\big|\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\big|W^{\prime\prime\prime}\big|*\big|\overline{M}\big|(s)\,ds+\frac{t}{c}\cdot\frac{2}{n}+\frac{1}{c}\int_{0}^{t}\big|Z\big|*\bar{\nu}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{c}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{s-r}^{\infty}\big|\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{1}(r)\}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{2}(r)\}}\big|N_{\nu}(dr,dz,dy)\,ds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{c}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}f^{\prime}_{n}\big(Z(s)\big)\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{1}(s)\}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{z\leq X_{2}(s)\}}\big)B_{c}(ds,dz).

Taking expectations on both sides and then using Fubini’s theorem and Young’s inequality for convolution,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[f_{n}\big(Z(t)\big)\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\bigg(\big|W^{\prime\prime}(0+)\big|+\int_{0}^{t}\big|W^{\prime\prime\prime}\big|(r)dr\bigg)\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\overline{M}(s)\big|\Big]\,ds+\frac{2\bar{\bar{\nu}}(0)}{c}\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|Z(s)\big|\Big]\,ds+\frac{t}{c}\cdot\frac{2}{n}.

By using the monotone convergence theorem along with the fact that
f_{n}(z)\rightarrow|z|
non-decreasingly as
n\to\infty
, we have uniformly in
x\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|Z(t)\big|\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\overline{M}(s)\big|+\big|Z(s)\big|\Big]\,ds.

(5.8)

On the other hand, by (
5.4
) we also have

\displaystyle\overline{M}(t)=c\cdot Z(t)-c\cdot\int_{0}^{t}\Big(W^{\prime\prime}(0+)\cdot\overline{H}(s)+W^{\prime\prime\prime}*\overline{M}(s)\Big)\,ds.

By using the triangle inequality and then Young’s inequality for convolution,

\displaystyle\big|\overline{M}(t)\big|\leq c\cdot\big|Z(t)\big|+c\cdot\Big(\big|W^{\prime\prime}(0+)\big|+\int_{0}^{t}\big|W^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)\big|\,dr\Big)\int_{0}^{t}\big|\overline{M}(s)\big|\,ds.

Taking expectations on both sides induces that for any
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\overline{M}(t)\big|\Big]\leq c\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|Z(t)\big|\Big]+C\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\overline{M}(s)\big|\Big]\,ds\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\overline{M}(s)\big|+\big|Z(s)\big|\Big]\,ds,

uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
.
Here the second inequality follows from (
5.8
).
Finally, combining this together with (
5.8
) yields that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|Z(t)\big|+\big|\overline{M}(t)\big|\Big]\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\overline{M}(s)\big|+\big|Z(s)\big|\Big]\,ds,\quad t\in[0,T].

By Grönwall’s inequality, we have
\mathbf{E}\big[|Z(t)|+|\overline{M}(t)|\big]=0

and hence
X_{1}(t)\overset{\rm a.s.}{=}X_{2}(t)
for any
t\in[0,T]
.
Finally, the continuity of
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
induces that they are indistinguishable.

\Box

6
Moment estimates and equivalent representations

In this section we first prove the moment estimates given in Theorem
1.8
and then establish a general equivalent representation of (
1.9
) that yields Theorem
1.6
as a corollary.

6.1
Proof of Theorem
1.8

The proof is carried out based on the equivalent equation (
1.16
) that, for convention, is written as

\displaystyle X_{\zeta}(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{i}(t),\quad t\geq 0.

(6.1)

Taking expectations on both sides and then by using the non-negativity of
X_{\zeta}
, we first have

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\big[X_{\zeta}(t)\big]=\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{i}(t)=\zeta\cdot\big(1-bW(t)\big)\leq\zeta,

(6.2)

uniformly in
\zeta,t\geq 0
and hence (
1.25
) hold for
p=1
.
By induction, we now proceed under the assumption that (
1.25
) holds for some
p\geq 1
and prove that it also holds for
2p
.
By the power inequality and (
6.1
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{i}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big],

(6.3)

for some constant
C>0
depending only on
p
.
The inequality in (
6.2
) tells that

\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{1}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]=\sup_{t\geq 0}\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{1}(t)\big|^{2p}\leq\zeta^{2p}.

Applying (
2.7
) to
\mathbf{E}\big[|\mathbf{I}_{2}(t)|^{2p}\big]
and then using (
1.12
) and (
1.14
), there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
p
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}_{2}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\bigg|\zeta\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|W(t)-W(t-y)\big|^{2}\,\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy\bigg|^{p}

\displaystyle+C\cdot\zeta\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|W(t)-W(t-y)\big|^{2p}\,\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\big|\zeta\cdot W(t)\big|^{p}\cdot\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(t)-W(t-y)\big)\,\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy\bigg|^{p}

\displaystyle+C\cdot\zeta\cdot\big|W(t)\big|^{2p-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(t)-W(t-y)\big)\,\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot|\zeta|^{p}\cdot\big|W(t)\big|^{p}+C\cdot\zeta\cdot\big|W(t)\big|^{2p-1}.

Applying (
2.5
) to
\mathbf{E}\big[|\mathbf{I}_{3}(t)|^{2p}\big]
and then using (
1.25
) give that uniformly in
\zeta,t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}_{3}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{p}\Big]\cdot\bigg|c\int_{0}^{t}\big|W^{\prime}(s)\big|^{2}ds\bigg|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{p}\Big]\cdot\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}W^{\prime}(s)ds\bigg|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{p}\Big]\cdot\big|W(t)\big|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\big(\zeta\vee\zeta^{p}\big)\cdot\big(1+W(t)\big)^{3p-2}.

Finally, an application of (
2.7
) to
\mathbf{E}\big[|\mathbf{I}_{4}(t)|^{2p}\big]
and then using (
1.25
) gives that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}_{4}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{p}\Big]\cdot\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(s)-W(s-y)\big)^{2}\,\nu(dy)\bigg|^{p}

(6.5)

\displaystyle+C\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\big[X_{\zeta}(s)\big]\cdot\int_{0}^{t}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(s)-W(s-y)\big)^{2p}\,\nu(dy)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{p}\Big]\cdot\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(s)-W(s-y)\big)^{2}\,\nu(dy)\bigg|^{p}

(6.7)

\displaystyle+C\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\big[X_{\zeta}(s)\big]\cdot\big|W(t)\big|^{2p-2}\cdot\int_{0}^{t}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(s)-W(s-y)\big)^{2}\,\nu(dy).

By using (
1.7
) and the monotonicity of
W
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W(s)-W(s-y)\big)^{2}\,\nu(dy)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}ds\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(W(s)\wedge\frac{y}{c}\Big)\big(W(s)-W(s-y)\big)\,\nu(dy)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(W(t)\wedge\frac{y}{c}\Big)\int_{0}^{t}\big(W(s)-W(s-y)\big)\,ds\,\nu(dy)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(W(t)\wedge\frac{y}{c}\Big)\int_{t-y}^{t}W(s)\,ds\,\nu(dy)

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\frac{W(t)}{c}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\big(cW(t)y\big)\wedge y^{2}\Big)\,\nu(dy),

which can be uniformly bounded by
c^{-2}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}(y\wedge y^{2})\,\nu(dy)
if
cW(t)\leq 1
, or by

\displaystyle\frac{W(t)}{c}\int_{0}^{1}y^{2}\,\nu(dy)+\frac{W(t)}{c}\int_{1}^{\infty}cW(t)y\,\nu(dy)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle|W(t)|^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(y\wedge y^{2})\,\nu(dy).

if
cW(t)>1
.
Plugging this into (
6.5
) and then using (
1.25
) induces that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\mathbf{I}_{4}(t)\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{p}\Big]\cdot\Big(1+W(t)\Big)^{2p}

\displaystyle+C\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\big[X_{\zeta}(s)\big]\cdot|W(t)|^{2p-2}\cdot\Big(1+W(t)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\big(\zeta\vee\zeta^{p}\big)\cdot\Big(1+W(t)\Big)^{4p-2}+C\cdot\zeta\cdot\Big(1+W(t)\Big)^{2p}

Taking all preceding estimates back into (
6.3
), we see that (
1.25
) also holds for
2p
.

6.2
Equivalent representations

We now provide an equivalent representation of (
1.9
) by using the stochastic Fubini’s theorems given in Proposition
2.6
and the next proposition that can be found in e.g.
[
35
, p.36]
or Lemma 3 in
[
7
]
.

Proposition 6.1

Consider two functions
g,h\in L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
.
Let
R_{g}\in L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathbb{R})
be the resolvent of the second kind of
g
defined as in (
3.3
).
The linear Volterra equation

\displaystyle f(t)=h(t)+g*f(t),\quad t\geq 0,

has a unique locally integrable solution.
Moreover, the solution has the representation

\displaystyle f(t)=h(t)+R_{g}*h(t),\quad t\geq 0.

For any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, recall the scaled function
W_{\beta}
defined above Lemma
2.4
.
By (
1.7
),

\displaystyle W^{\prime}_{\beta}(0)=\frac{1}{c}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(x)\leq\frac{1}{c}.

(6.8)

Lemma 6.2

For any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, the stochastic Volterra equation (
1.9
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle X_{\zeta}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\zeta\cdot c\cdot W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t)+\int_{0}^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W_{\beta}(t)-W_{\beta}(t-y)\big)\,N_{0}(dz,dy)

(6.11)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}(\beta-b)\cdot W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t-s)X_{\zeta}(s)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t-s)\,B_{c}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W_{\beta}(t-s)-W_{\beta}(t-s-y)\big)\,\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy),\quad t\geq 0.

Proof.
We just prove that any solution of (
6.11
) also solves (
1.9
). The converse can be proved similarly.
For convention, we write (
6.11
) as

\displaystyle X_{\zeta}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathbf{I}_{\beta,i}(t)+(\beta-b)W^{\prime}_{\beta}*X_{\zeta}(t),\quad t\geq 0,

(6.12)

with
\mathbf{I}_{\beta,i}(t)
,
i=1,\cdots,4
representing the other four terms on the right-hand side of (
1.20
) sequentially.
Multiplying both sides of the identity in Lemma
2.4
by
\beta-b
gives that

\displaystyle(\beta-b)W^{\prime}(t)=(\beta-b)W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t)+\big((\beta-b)W^{\prime}\big)*\big((\beta-b)W^{\prime}_{\beta}\big)(t),\quad t\geq 0.

Armed with this equality, we apply Proposition
6.1
to (
6.12
) and get

\displaystyle X_{\zeta}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{4}\Big(\mathbf{I}_{\beta,i}(t)+(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*\mathbf{I}_{\beta,i}(t)\Big),\quad t\geq 0.

Comparing this to (
1.9
) or (
4.149
), it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{i}(t)=\mathbf{I}_{\beta,i}(t)+(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*\mathbf{I}_{\beta,i}(t),\quad i=1,\cdots,4.

(6.13)

\bullet

For
i=1
, by Lemma
2.4
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\beta,i}(t)+(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*\mathbf{I}_{\beta,i}(t)=\zeta\cdot c\cdot\big(W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t)+(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t)\big)=\zeta\cdot c\cdot W^{\prime}(t)=\mathbf{I}_{1}(t).

\bullet

For
i=2
, the non-negativity of
W^{\prime}
and
W^{\prime}_{\beta}
allows us to use Fubini’s theorem pathwisely to obtain that

\displaystyle(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*\mathbf{I}_{\beta,2}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}(t-r)\int_{0}^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{r-y}^{r}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(s)\,ds\Big)\,N_{0}(dz,dy)\,dr

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}(t-r)\int_{r-y}^{r}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(s)\,ds\,dr\Big)\,N_{0}(dz,dy).

Additionally, using the change of variables and then Fubini’s theorem again to the integrand in the last stochastic integral gives that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}(t-r)\int_{r-y}^{r}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(s)\,ds\,dr\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}(t-r)\int_{0}^{y}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(r-s)\,ds\,dr

(6.14)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y}\int_{0}^{t}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}(t-r)W^{\prime}_{\beta}(r-s)\,dr\,ds

(6.15)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t-s)\,ds

(6.16)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{t-y}^{t}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*W^{\prime}_{\beta}(s)\,ds.

(6.17)

Combining these together and then using the identity in Lemma
2.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\beta,2}(t)+(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*\mathbf{I}_{\beta,2}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\int_{t-y}^{t}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(s)\,ds+\int_{t-y}^{t}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*W^{\prime}_{\beta}(s)\,ds\Big)\,N_{0}(dz,dy)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{t-y}^{t}W^{\prime}(s)\,ds\Big)\,N_{0}(dz,dy),

and hence (
6.13
) holds for
i=2
.

\bullet

For
i=3
, by (
1.7
) and (
6.8
) we first have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\beta-b|W^{\prime}(t-r)\cdot\int_{0}^{r}\big|W^{\prime}_{\beta}(s)\big|^{2}\,ds\,dr\leq\frac{|\beta-b|}{c^{3}}\cdot t^{2},

which along with Theorem
1.8
indicates that conditions in Proposition
2.6
are satisfied. Using the identity (
2.8
) gives that

\displaystyle(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*\mathbf{I}_{\beta,3}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}(t-r)\int_{0}^{r}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(r-s)\,B_{c}(ds,dz)\,dr

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t-s)\,B_{c}(ds,dz).

Similarly as in the preceding argument, we use the identity in Lemma
2.4
again to get

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\beta,3}(t)+(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*\mathbf{I}_{\beta,3}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\Big(W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t-s)+(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t-s)\Big)\,B_{c}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}W^{\prime}(t-s)\,B_{c}(ds,dz),

and hence (
6.13
) holds for
i=3
.

\bullet

For
i=4
, similarly as in the preceding case, by using (
6.8
) and (
1.2
) we also have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}(t-r)\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big|\int_{r-y}^{r}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(u)\,du\Big|^{2}\nu(dy)\,dr\leq\frac{t}{c^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}(t\wedge y)^{2}\nu(dy)<\infty,

and hence conditions in Proposition
2.6
are satisfied.
By (
2.9
), we have
(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*\mathbf{I}_{\beta,4}(t)
equals to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}(t-r)\int_{0}^{r}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{r-s-y}^{r-s}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(u)\,du\Big)\,\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy)\,dr

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{0}^{t-s}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}(t-s-r)\int_{r-y}^{r}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(u)\,du\,dr\Big)\,\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{t-s-y}^{t-s}(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*W^{\prime}_{\beta}(r)dr\Big)\,\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy)

Here the last equality is obtained similarly as in (
6.14
).
Again, combining this with
\mathbf{I}_{\beta,4}(t)
and then using the identity in Lemma
2.4
gives that

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{\beta,4}(t)+(\beta-b)W^{\prime}*\mathbf{I}_{\beta,4}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{t-s-y}^{t-s}W^{\prime}(r)dr\Big)\,\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy),

and hence (
6.13
) holds for
i=4
.

\Box

7
Hölder regularity and maximal inequality

This section devotes to the proof of Theorem
1.9
by using the next equivalent representation of (
1.9
)

\displaystyle X_{\zeta}(t)\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\zeta\Big(1-\beta\cdot W_{\beta}(t)\Big)+\int_{0}^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W_{\beta}(t)-W_{\beta}(t-y)\big)\,\widetilde{N}_{0}(dz,dy)

(7.3)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}(\beta-b)\cdot W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t-s)X_{\zeta}(s)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t-s)\,B_{c}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(W_{\beta}(t-s)-W_{\beta}(t-s-y)\big)\,\widetilde{N}_{\nu}(ds,dz,dy),\quad t\geq 0.

It can be identified similarly as in Remark
1.3
.
For convention, we denote by
\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,i}(t)
with
i=1,\cdots,5
the five terms on the right side of
(
7.3
).
By the Kolmogorov continuity theorem; see e.g. Theorem 2.1 in
[
62
, p.26]
,
it suffices to identify that they satisfy certain constraints on the moments of their increments.

Proposition 7.1

We have uniformly in
\zeta\geq 0
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,1}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,1}(t_{1})\Big|\leq\zeta\cdot\frac{|\beta|}{c}\cdot\big|t_{2}-t_{1}\big|.

(7.4)

Proof.
By (
6.8
), the scale function
W_{\beta}
enjoys the global Lipschitz continuity, i.e.,

\displaystyle\big|W_{\beta}(t_{2})-W_{\beta}(t_{1})\big|=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}W^{\prime}(r)dr\leq\frac{|t_{2}-t_{1}|}{c},

uniformly in
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}
and hence the desired upper bound (
7.4
) follows.

\Box

Proposition 7.2

For each
p\geq 1
and
\beta>0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
\zeta,t\geq 0
and
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq t
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,2}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,2}(t_{1})\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot(\zeta\vee\zeta^{p})\cdot\big(|t_{2}-t_{1}|\wedge 1\big)^{p}.

Proof.
By the definition of
\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,2}
, we first have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,2}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,2}(t_{1})\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\zeta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{t_{2}-y}^{t_{2}}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(r)dr-\int_{t_{1}-y}^{t_{1}}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(r)dr\Big)\widetilde{N}_{0}(dz,dy).

An application of (
2.7
) gives that for some constant
C>0
depending only on
p
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,2}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,2}(t_{1})\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\Big(\zeta^{p}\cdot\big|A_{2}(t_{1},t_{2})\big|^{p}+\zeta\cdot A_{2p}(t_{1},t_{2})\Big),

(7.5)

where

\displaystyle A_{2k}(t_{1},t_{2}):=\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big|\int_{t_{2}-y}^{t_{2}}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(r)dr-\int_{t_{1}-y}^{t_{1}}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(r)dr\Big|^{2k}\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy,\quad k\geq 1.

The upper bound in (
6.8
) tells that uniformly in
y,t\geq 0
and
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq t
,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{t_{2}-y}^{t_{2}}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(r)dr-\int_{t_{1}-y}^{t_{1}}W^{\prime}_{\beta}(r)dr\Big|\leq\frac{|t_{2}-t_{1}|\wedge y}{c}\wedge W_{\beta}(t).

Then there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
\zeta
and
t,t_{1},t_{2}
such that

\displaystyle A_{2k}(t_{1},t_{2})\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(|t_{2}-t_{1}|\wedge y\wedge W_{\beta}(t)\big)^{2k}\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(|t_{2}-t_{1}|\wedge y\wedge W_{\beta}(t)\big)\bar{\nu}(y)\,dy\cdot\big||t_{2}-t_{1}|\wedge W_{\beta}(t)\big|^{2k-1}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\big(1+|t_{2}-t_{1}|\wedge W_{\beta}(t)\big)\cdot\big||t_{2}-t_{1}|\wedge W_{\beta}(t)\big|^{2k-1}.

Plugging this with
k=1
and
p
back into (
7.5
) gives that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,2}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,2}(t_{1})\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\zeta^{p}\cdot\big(1+|t_{2}-t_{1}|\wedge W_{\beta}(t)\big)^{p}\cdot\big||t_{2}-t_{1}|\wedge W_{\beta}(t)\big|^{p}

\displaystyle+C\cdot\zeta\cdot\big(1+|t_{2}-t_{1}|\wedge W_{\beta}(t)\big)\cdot\big||t_{2}-t_{1}|\wedge W_{\beta}(t)\big|^{2p-1},

which along with the fact that
\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}W_{\beta}(t)\leq(\beta+b)^{-1}
yields the desired upper bound follows.

\Box

Proposition 7.3

For each
p\geq 1
and
\beta>0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
t\geq 0
and
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq t
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,3}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,3}(t_{1})\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\big(\zeta\vee\zeta^{2p}\big)\cdot\big(1+W(t)\big)^{4p-2}\cdot t^{p}\cdot|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{p}.

Proof.
By the definition of
\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,3}
and Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\Big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,3}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,3}(t_{1})\Big|^{2p}\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle|\beta|^{2p}\cdot\bigg|\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\big(W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t_{2}-s)-W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t_{1}-s)\big)X_{\zeta}(s)ds\bigg|^{2p}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle|\beta|^{2p}\cdot\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t_{2}-s)-W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t_{1}-s)\big)^{2}\,ds\bigg|^{p}\cdot\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{2}\,ds\bigg|^{p}.

Note that the claim in Lemma
2.2
also holds for
W^{\prime}_{\beta}
, then uniformly in
t_{2}>t_{1}\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t_{2}-s)-W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t_{1}-s)\big)^{2}\,ds\leq C\cdot|t_{2}-t_{1}|.

(7.6)

Consequently, there exists a constant
C>0
depending only in
p
and
\beta
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,3}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,3}(t_{1})\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{p}\cdot\mathbf{E}\bigg[\Big|\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{2}\,ds\Big|^{p}\bigg].

Using Hölder’s inequality, Fubini’s theorem and (
1.25
) to the last expectation,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\bigg[\Big|\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{2}\,ds\Big|^{p}\bigg]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\bigg[|t_{2}|^{p-1}\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{2p}\,ds\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle t^{p}\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot\big(\zeta\vee\zeta^{2p}\big)\cdot\big(1+W(t)\big)^{4p-2}\cdot t^{p}.

The desired upper bounds follows by combining all estimates above together.

\Box

Proposition 7.4

For each
p\geq 1
and
\beta>0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
\zeta,t\geq 0
and
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq t
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,4}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,4}(t_{1})\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C\cdot(\zeta\vee\zeta^{p})\cdot\big(1+W(t)\big)^{2p-2}\cdot|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{p}.

Proof.
The fact that
W^{\prime}_{\beta}(x)=0
if
x<0
allows us to write
\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,4}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,4}(t_{1})
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,4}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,4}(t_{1})\!\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\!\!

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\int_{0}^{X_{\zeta}(s)}\big(W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t_{2}-s)-W^{\prime}_{\zeta}(t_{1}-s)\big)\,B_{c}(ds,dz).

By using (
2.5
), there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
p
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,4}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}_{\beta,4}(t_{1})\big|^{2p}\Big]\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!

\displaystyle C\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,t_{2}]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\big|X_{\zeta}(s)\big|^{p}\Big]\cdot\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t_{2}-s)-W^{\prime}_{\beta}(t_{1}-s)\big)^{2}\,ds\bigg|^{p},

which can be bounded by
C\cdot(\zeta\vee\zeta^{p})\cdot\big(1+W(t)\big)^{2p-2}\cdot|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{p}
uniformly in
\zeta,t\geq 0
and
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq t
; see (
1.25
) and (
7.6
).

\Box

Proposition 7.5

For each
p\geq 1
and
\beta>0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
\zeta,t\geq 0
and
